Disabled options (including options under a disabled optgroup), explicit empty values, invalid numbers, out-of-range values, and duplicate pixel positions are excluded. The focused range tick WPT files pass 4/4; Goosie and Chromium rendered all four tick pixels at x=57, 74, 90, and 102 in the 800×600 comparison. Reftest `mismatch` evaluation now treats any per-channel pixel mismatch above the comparator tolerance as a mismatch, independently of the configured match threshold; unit coverage confirms exact equality fails `mismatch` and a changed pixel passes. The latest input aggregate reports 167 candidates, 161 passed, 5 skipped, and 1 timed out. Both range tick reftests pass; the reset-restoration WPT also passes after the popup browsing-context work below. Broader input and HTML conformance remain open.

**`window.open()` child navigation and form restoration checked 2026-10-28:** `window.open()` now creates a same-origin child browsing context backed by the iframe document loader and returns a stable WindowProxy. The proxy exposes its document, opener, location, close state, and child session history. Relative `Location.href` assignments from the opener realm resolve against the opener document URL, following the Location href setter's entry-settings-object base. Child session history records navigations and retains prior document trees so back navigation restores changed controls without dispatching user `input` or `change` events. A Go regression covers the opened proxy, opener, initial child document, relative navigation, history length/back, and restored range value. The pinned WPT `range-restore-oninput-onchange-event.https.html` passes both range and text cases (1/1 file). The latest input aggregate reports 167 candidates, 161 passed, 5 skipped, and 1 timed out; the cross-origin picker iframe remains open. This is partial auxiliary browsing-context and history support, not full navigation conformance.

**Input numeric values and stepping checked 2026-10-28:** valid number strings preserve their authored floating-point form, while invalid syntax (including a trailing decimal point) sanitizes to empty. `valueAsNumber` converts the date/time/month/week/number/range value spaces, including milliseconds for date/week, month indices, and midnight-wrapping time values; invalid values return `NaN`, non-finite writes throw `TypeError`, and unsupported states throw `InvalidStateError`. `stepUp()`/`stepDown()` apply each state’s default step and scale, parse valid positive step values, align mismatched values to the step base, respect minimum/maximum boundaries, and leave values unchanged when the bounds are inverted. Decimal range serialization follows step precision. The pinned WPT selection `input-stepup.html`, both stepdown files, datetime/week/month, time, range-2, floating-step/realignment, and valueAsNumber stepping passes 11/11; the dedicated valueAsNumber getter/setter, NaN, TypeError, and InvalidStateError files pass 4/4; `number.html` and `range.html` pass 2/2. Go regressions cover numeric values, date conversion, decimal stepping, and minimum handling. More input-state edge cases and full HTML conformance remain open.

**Input valueAsDate checked 2026-10-28:** `valueAsDate` now exposes UTC Date objects for date, month, week, and time states, converts writes from Date objects to each state’s value format, clears applicable values when assigned `null`, returns `null` for empty/invalid/unsupported values, throws `TypeError` for non-Date writes, and throws `InvalidStateError` for unsupported input types. The dedicated WPT files for getters/setters, stepping, null clearing, TypeError, InvalidStateError, and datetime-local all pass 6/6. Go regressions cover epoch conversion and null clearing. Other input state and full HTML conformance remain open.

**Input datalist IDL checked 2026-10-28:** `HTMLInputElement.list` resolves the current `list` attribute to the first matching HTML datalist and returns `null` for a missing, empty, or non-datalist target. The getter is live and read-only. The pinned `input-list.html` WPT passes 1/1; a Go regression checks datalist lookup and missing targets. Tree-scope-specific datalist resolution and remaining input/HTML conformance are still open.

**EventTarget interface and listener dispatch checked 2026-10-28:** script-created `EventTarget` instances inherit `addEventListener`, `removeEventListener`, and `dispatchEvent`. Generic, node, and Window listener records support `once`, `passive`, and AbortSignal cleanup; listener removal matches callback identity and capture only. Passive callbacks cannot cancel through `preventDefault()` or legacy `returnValue`. Event phases/constants, `Event`/`CustomEvent`, legacy `Document.createEvent()`/`initEvent()`, at-target `composedPath()`, dispatch-state errors, callback `this` binding, and dynamic `handleEvent` lookup are implemented. Event invocation uses listener snapshots, with a shared removed marker so listeners removed during an active iteration are skipped and newly added listeners wait until the next dispatch. Event propagation binds pre-dispatch `cancelBubble`/stop flags into dispatch, connects active cancelBubble changes to propagation, restores flags and script methods after dispatch, and updates `srcElement` with `target`; target capture stops the subsequent target bubble group while allowing remaining capture listeners. Script-created `Event`/`KeyboardEvent` constructors require `new` and required type arguments; Event has a positive timestamp and KeyboardEvent defaults `location` to zero. The selected pinned WPT set passes 25/25 files: the 11 EventTarget core cases, `Event-initEvent.html`, `Event-init-while-dispatching.html`, `Event-isTrusted.any.js`, `Event-dispatch-omitted-capture.html`, `Event-dispatch-reenter.html`, `Event-dispatch-redispatch.html`, `Event-cancelBubble.html`, `Event-dispatch-bubble-canceled.html`, `Event-defaultPrevented-after-dispatch.html`, `CustomEvent.html`, `Event-constructors.any.js`, `Event-propagation.html`, and `Event-stopPropagation-cancel-bubbling.html`, and `remove-all-listeners.html`; report: `/tmp/goosie-task55-event-conformance-final/results.json`. The listener-removal regression also passes independently (1/1) at `/tmp/goosie-task55-listener-removal-current/results.json`; a Go regression covers removing a later listener, adding its replacement, and dispatching again. A full pinned directory checkpoint covering `dom/events`, passive wheel/touch cases, and scrolling reports 54/172 runnable passed, 93 failed, 25 errored, and 6 skipped; this was collected before the final two propagation corrections, and records substantial remaining activation, event-global, passive input, wheel/touch, and scrolling gaps. Report: `/tmp/goosie-task55-dom-events-current/results.json`. Go regressions cover listener snapshot mutation and removal, pre-dispatch and in-listener cancelBubble, stopPropagation method reuse, passive cancellation, one-shot behavior, AbortSignal cleanup, and testdriver trusted mouse/click redispatch. Focused package tests, scoped vet, curator validation, architecture checks, and `git diff --check` pass. This does not measure all DOM events or HTML Living Standard behavior; TASK-055 remains incomplete.

**Legacy `window.event` dispatch state checked 2026-10-28:** `window.event` now starts as an own `undefined` property, tracks the current event through node, generic EventTarget, Window, XHR, and FileReader callbacks, restores outer state after nested dispatch, preserves author replacement, and is suppressed for listeners inside shadow trees. AbortSignal and Performance now expose generic EventTarget methods. Iframe navigation dispatches `beforeunload` before replacing the child document, and `onbeforeunload` return values are string-coerced while the event remains current. The iframe WindowProxy exposes the child realm's `Function` constructor and `event` property; imported callback execution carries event context and reports uncaught callback exceptions into that realm. Cross-realm Error copies retain inherited standard `name` and `message` values. A Go regression covers generic-target dispatch, nested restoration, initial/replaced values, and author replacement. The pinned focused event-global selection now passes 6/6 files (up from 2/6 before this slice); report: `/tmp/goosie-task55-window-event-wpt/results.json`. Scoped JS and architecture tests pass; TASK-055 remains incomplete.

**Input associated labels checked 2026-10-28:** `HTMLInputElement.labels` returns a live NodeList of label elements whose `for` or descendant association resolves to that input. The pinned `input-labels.html` WPT passes, including nested labels. Detached-tree and shadow-tree label association remain open.

**Date/time input sanitization checked 2026-10-28:** `datetime-local` values accept the standard space separator and serialize to `T` form with canonical time precision; invalid seconds/fractions are rejected, including time fractions longer than three digits. The pinned `datetime-local.html`, `datetime-local-trailing-zeros.html`, and `time-2.html` files pass 3/3. Broader temporal input edge cases remain open.

**Email input IDN values checked 2026-10-28:** single and `multiple` email values now trim their state-specific whitespace and map Unicode domains to ASCII using the IDNA lookup profile while retaining local parts. This covers parser-created values, dynamic attribute writes, value setters, and transitions into the email state. The focused `email-value-idn.html` and `email.html` WPT files pass 2/2. Email editing behavior with test-driver keyboard input and additional IDNA/email edge cases remain open.

**Hidden `_charset_` submission checked 2026-10-28:** successful hidden controls named `_charset_` under ASCII case-insensitive matching submit the selected encoding label, including canonical `UTF-8`; non-ASCII lookalikes and non-hidden controls preserve their values. The focused form-data regression and pinned `hidden-charset-case-sensitive.html` WPT pass; this fix is included in the 113/148 current full input run above.

**Mutability pseudo-classes checked 2026-10-28:** `:read-write` now follows HTML mutability for inputs whose states support `readonly`, textareas, and editable content; disabled fieldset descendants are read-only. The CSS regressions cover dynamic fieldset disabled state, readonly inputs, checkbox readonly non-applicability, textarea, contenteditable, and default read-only elements. The pinned `input-disabled-fieldset-dynamic.html` WPT passes and is included in the latest input aggregate above.

**Blur/change tracking and testdriver text entry checked 2026-10-28:** text-control blur dispatches `change` only when the user changed the value during the focus period and the final value differs from its focus-time value; script-only writes do not count, and script writes from `input` handlers do not erase the user-edit marker. The WPT `send_keys` shim now replaces selected text with `setRangeText()` and reports its simulated edit to the session. The regression covers script-only mutation, user edits restored to the focus-time value, and a final user edit that must dispatch `change`. The pinned `change-set-value.html` passes with the related charset and read-only WPTs (3/3), and is included in the latest input aggregate above.

**WPT special-key text entry checked 2026-10-28:** the testdriver shim maps WebDriver's private-use Backspace/Delete/Enter keys to keyboard key names, applies Backspace/Delete to a selection or adjacent UTF-16 code unit, and skips edits for disabled or readonly controls. When a text control does not expose selection APIs, the shim falls back to appending typed characters to the current value. The pinned `change-to-empty-value.html`, `password-delete-space.html`, `date-readonly-keyboard.html`, `maxlength-number.html`, `change-set-value.html`, hidden charset, disabled-fieldset, and radio-disconnected-group-owner files pass 8/8; `reset.html` and `files.html` also pass in the latest full run. The 113/148 full report includes the special-key fallback and radio grouping fix.


**Table form-pointer association checked 2026-10-28:** the parser's in-table form start rule now sets the form element pointer and removes the form from the open-elements stack; the end tag clears that pointer. This associates controls parsed inside table markup with the form without incorrectly nesting them in the form element. A Go regression checks the parser-assigned owner and tree structure. The pinned `reset.html` WPT now passes all 6 subtests, including reset of the table-associated input; it is included in the latest 113/148 full input run.

**FileList identity checked 2026-10-28:** `input.files` keeps the assigned FileList object, and `DataTransfer.files` returns a stable FileList object, so controls can share the same list by identity. Assigning `null` leaves the current list unchanged; assigning an array throws `TypeError`. Clearing a file input or resetting its form clears the shared list in place and updates the associated controls' file state. Go regressions cover File/DataTransfer/FormData identity, null/array assignment, list sharing, and clearing a shared selection. The pinned `files.html` WPT passes all 24 subtests and is included in the latest 113/148 full input run.

**`Element.after()` checked 2026-10-28:** `Element`, character-data, and doctype nodes now expose `after()` with node/string argument conversion, ordered fragment insertion, quota admission, and mutation/custom-element notifications. The Go regression covers mixed node, string, and fragment arguments. This unblocked the WPT's DOM setup and, with the keyboard action-sequence shim below, enabled the radio keyboard navigation WPT to pass.

**WPT action sequences checked 2026-10-28:** the injected testdriver shim processes action sources tick by tick and retains held keyboard and pointer state across action calls. It supports keyboard sources with held modifiers, Tab focus navigation, radio arrow navigation, Enter and Space activation, pauses, and mouse pointer move/down/up. It exposes held activation state through the WPT host and clears it on key release or focus movement. The focused five-file run passes `radio-keyboard-navigation-order.html`, `checkable-active-space-key-being-disabled.html`, `checkable-active-space-key-prevented-default.html`, `checkable-active-onblur.html`, and `checkable-active-onblur-with-click.html` (5/5). The latest fresh 148-file run reports 135 pass, 0 fail, 13 errors, and 0 skipped (91.2%), up from the earlier 113/148 checkpoint. The remaining errors are tracked in the input-suite summary above. This WPT adapter exercises DOM event behavior but does not yet provide trusted platform input events or general wheel/touch action sources.

**Radio group sequential focus navigation and focus scrolling checked 2026-10-28:** Tab order treats a named radio group as one stop, using its checked enabled member or its first enabled member when none is checked. A programmatically focused non-selected radio advances to the group’s sequential stop. Focusing an off-screen control scrolls it into view; `document.scrollingElement` returns the root element (or body in quirks mode), and its `scrollTop` shares the window scroll offset. Go regressions cover radio-group Tab ordering and root/window scroll synchronization. All five focused radio-focus-navigation WPT files now pass, including off-screen radio scrolling. The latest input aggregate reports 167 candidates, 161 passed, 5 skipped, and 1 timed out; it includes this focus-scroll change.

**Testdriver click and number editing follow-up checked 2026-10-28:** the WPT click adapter focuses and activates elements directly so headless tests do not fail browser paint-tree hit testing; HTMLElement anchor activation now suppresses `javascript:` navigation within editable content. The number adapter retains a caret-aware editing buffer, rejects invalid scientific-notation insertions, normalizes a user-entered trailing decimal point, and sets `ValidityState.badInput` when user-entered text cannot be represented as a number; script assignments continue to sanitize without setting `badInput`. Go validity regression covers the distinction between script-set and user-edit bad-input state. The focused anchor and number selection passes 4/4, including `anchor-active-contenteditable.html`, `anchor-contenteditable-navigate.html`, `number-constraint-validation.html`, and `number-invalid-scientific-key-events.html`. A refreshed 148-file input run reports 128 pass, 6 fail, 14 errors, and 0 skipped (86.5%); the prior 123/148 report is superseded. The testdriver click adapter exercises synthetic DOM activation, not trusted platform pointer input, and overall input and HTML conformance remain open.

**Email input editing and select() checked 2026-10-28:** simulated user editing now retains the unsanitized visible text while the input value getter returns the whitespace-trimmed email value. `input.select()` exposes the selected email control text through `window.getSelection().toString()`, while an assignment through `.value` clears the edit display buffer. Go regression covers user-edited and script-set selection text. The pinned `email-set-value.html` passes with the click and number editing selection (5/5); `email.html` and `email-value-idn.html` also pass in the latest input aggregate, which reports 167 candidates, 161 passed, 5 skipped, and 1 timed out. This behavior does not close email state or full HTML conformance.

**Input pattern Unicode Sets follow-up checked 2026-10-28:** HTML pattern validation translates ASCII hexadecimal property sets and basic emoji sequence patterns, supports tested character-set subtraction and `\q{}` string alternatives, and treats invalid Unicode Sets character-class syntax as an ignored pattern. Go regression covers positive property matches, set subtraction/string alternatives, and invalid-pattern handling. The pinned `pattern_attribute.html` and `pattern_attribute_v_flag.html` pass, and the focused seven-file click/email/number/pattern set passes 7/7. The latest input aggregate reports 167 candidates, 161 passed, 5 skipped, and 1 timed out. RGI emoji membership currently uses an approximate code-point/sequence expression rather than the full generated Unicode RGI string-property table; exact property coverage and overall HTML conformance remain open.

The initial inventory below is a historical starting point, not a complete statement of scope. Existing implementations and this document’s earlier “Out of Scope” list do not narrow the goal. HTML is now a Living Standard; finish criteria must cover the current standard and its Web Platform Test coverage, including dependent specifications where HTML delegates behavior to them.

**Dialog behavior checked 2026-10-27; parser checkpoint refreshed 2026-10-28:** the WPT curator now discovers `html/semantics/interactive-elements/the-dialog-element`. `dialog.close()` and `method=dialog` form submission queue a trusted `close` event; Escape closes the modal and queues the same trusted event after its cancel event. `showModal()` rejects disconnected dialogs with `InvalidStateError`. `requestClose()` dispatches a trusted, cancelable `cancel` event, preserves `returnValue` when canceled, and queues a trusted `close` event on success. `closedBy` reflects case-insensitive `any`, `closerequest`, and `none` values and computes the open-modal default; the `:modal` pseudo-class tracks modal dialog state. Promise now exposes generic `Promise.withResolvers()` with the standard method descriptor, and `addEventListener` uses an AbortSignal to remove the associated listener on abort. This fixed leaked cancel listeners across request-close subtests. The focused pinned dialog files `dialog-close-event-async.html`, `dialog-close.html`, `dialog-focusing-steps-disconnected.html`, `dialog-requestclose.html` (75/75 subtests), and `dialog-return-value.html` pass 5/5. Go regressions cover request-close cancellation, closedBy reflection, `:modal`, Promise resolution/rejection and subclass construction, and AbortSignal listener cleanup. `dialog-closedby.html` still times out waiting for `test_driver` keyboard/click automation, so that file's reflection assertions are not measured. The pinned `html/syntax/parsing` run checked 2026-10-28 reports 59/61 passing, 1 failing, 1 skipped, 0 errors. The sole failure is the tentative `DOMContentLoaded-defer-task-order.tentative.html`, whose timer-versus-DOMContentLoaded task ordering is explicitly unspecified; the skip is an optional pagehide-reentrancy case. The shadow-cycle adoption-agency test exposed infinite recursion in radio-group tree invalidation and shadow-root HTML serialization; both traversals now stop on repeated nodes, and the isolated WPT passes. Focused `internal/js` and `test/js` suites pass after the fix. This does not establish overall dialog or parser conformance.

**Dialog modal focus behavior checked 2026-10-28:** `show()` and `showModal()` now run dialog focusing steps, preferring the dialog’s own `autofocus`, then an autofocus descendant, then the first keyboard-focusable descendant, and finally the dialog. Document autofocus runs before the load event, and focus changes dispatch `focusin`/`focusout`. Closing restores the pre-open focus target, including Escape and `method=dialog`; when the saved node has been removed, focus falls back to the body. Document activeElement retargets through shadow hosts, and ShadowRoot.activeElement exposes the focused descendant. Focus restoration does not scroll; normal focus flushes pending layout, and window scroll changes dispatch scroll events. The runtime exposes viewport innerWidth/innerHeight from engine layout. Sequential Tab candidates are scoped to the topmost modal, programmatic focus outside it is rejected, and inert elements do not receive focus or hit-test results. Engine regressions cover autofocus precedence, close restoration, shadow-root restoration, removed-target fallback, Escape and `method=dialog` restoration, and Tab wrap/rejection; mutations disabling modal initial focus and enabling focus scrolling during dialog restoration are killed by the regressions (the latter makes `focus-after-close.html` fail with `scrollTop` 10 instead of 0). The focused pinned set `child-sequential-focus.html`, `dialog-focusability.html`, `dialog-focusing-steps-inert.html`, `dialog-inert.html`, `show-modal-focusing-steps.html`, and `focus-after-close.html` passes 6/6 files, report: `/tmp/goosie-task55-dialog-focus-final10/results.json`. Full dialog conformance remains open. The dialog cancel-events WPT currently cannot run because its WebIDL2 dependency is unavailable in the harness.

**Contenteditable DOM reflection and editing commands checked 2026-10-27:** HTML elements expose `contentEditable` with ASCII-case-insensitive `true`, `false`, and `plaintext-only` states, `inherit` for absent/invalid values, canonical lowercase setter reflection, and `SyntaxError` for invalid assignments. `isContentEditable` follows inherited states, including an explicit `false` boundary; contenteditable elements also participate in click and programmatic focus. `Document.getSelection()` returns the document's stable Selection object; `designMode` reflects `on`/`off` and affects inherited editability. `document.execCommand()` handles selected `delete`, `insertText`, `bold`, `italic`, and `underline` across multiple text nodes inside one editing host. `Range.deleteContents()` removes fully selected descendant nodes and selected portions of boundary text nodes, then collapses at the range start. `Range.extractContents()` and `Range.cloneContents()` preserve partial ancestor structure and boundary character data; extraction moves fully selected nodes, mutates partial character data, and collapses the range at its start. These editing paths use UTF-16 offsets; Range boundary comparison and `Range.toString()` handle tree-order boundaries across ancestor/descendant containers. The complete curated pinned contenteditable directory passes all 6 runnable files, including the enumerated-value, slotted inheritance/deletion, and designMode selection cases. Go regressions cover cross-node editing-command deletion, insertion, and formatting plus Range deletion and serialization, ancestor/descendant Range comparison, text replacement, formatting wrappers, range collapse, reflected values, inheritance, designMode, and invalid setters. `execCommand()` deletion of selected element nodes, collapsed-selection formatting toggles, nested formatting equivalence, caret rendering and pointer placement, full flat-tree inheritance, complete IME composition lifecycle, and nested editing behavior remain open.

**Contenteditable user text entry and IME preview checked 2026-10-27:** keyboard character entry, committed text, and insertion from the text/paste path target the focused editable host's live Selection through `execCommand("insertText")`. Backspace and forward Delete expand a collapsed text Selection by one Unicode scalar value when its surrogate pair is within a Text node and use the editing command's deletion path; a non-collapsed Selection is deleted. Left/Right move a collapsed caret in flattened text-tree order across Text node boundaries without splitting UTF-16 surrogate pairs; Home/End move it to the current Text node's boundary. Non-collapsed selections collapse to the corresponding edge. Text-control composition updates are reflected in their values, and the follow-up below now updates contenteditable Text nodes at collapsed Text carets; commit replaces the active passage. The JS event bridge constructs and dispatches `InputEvent`, reflects `data`, `inputType`, and `isComposing`, and preserves `instanceof InputEvent` and `instanceof Event`. Engine-generated beforeinput and input events for text insertion/deletion are trusted; `beforeinput` is cancelable and cancellation suppresses the edit. Focused JS and engine regressions verify subtype payloads, trust, insertion after caret movement across nodes, surrogate-pair-safe movement/deletion, backward/forward deletion, input-control forward deletion, composition update and commit, and cancellation. Clearing the trusted flag, changing forward deletion's `inputType` to backward, disabling rightward caret advancement, and zeroing the IME preview offset each made their engine regressions fail; restoring the implementations made them pass. `go test ./internal/dom ./internal/js ./test/dom ./test/js ./test/engine ./cmd/goosie ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining gaps include InputEvent.dataTransfer, grapheme-cluster and visual Up/Down caret navigation, pointer-based caret placement, deletion of surrogate pairs split across Text nodes, composition for noncollapsed/multi-node selections, underline/candidate rendering, rich Enter paragraph behavior, and nested editing behavior. Full contenteditable and HTML conformance remain open.

**Contenteditable composition event lifecycle checked 2026-10-27:** trusted `CompositionEvent` objects now expose `data`, preserve `instanceof CompositionEvent`/`UIEvent`/`Event`, and are emitted for start, update, commit, cancellation, and explicit focus loss. Composition updates at a collapsed Text caret now update the Text node between trusted non-cancelable `beforeinput`/`input` events with `inputType="insertCompositionText"`; commit replaces the active passage with `insertFromComposition`. Go regressions check repeated updates, trust and order, live DOM text, commit, and cancellation. Broader WPT event ordering and underline/candidate rendering remain unverified.

**Input and textarea composition events checked 2026-10-27:** focused input and textarea controls now emit trusted `compositionstart`, one `compositionupdate` per changed marked preview, and `compositionend` on commit or preview cancellation. A table-driven engine regression checks event order, data, trust, and committed value for both controls; suppressing compositionupdate makes both cases fail. At this checkpoint, commits and focus loss did not yet emit all required input events; the follow-up below closes those gaps for explicit commit and blur. This records the earlier event-lifecycle milestone; live value updates and paired input events are covered by the follow-up below. These checks are focused lifecycle coverage, not full Input Events or HTML conformance.

**Text-control composition commit and blur events checked 2026-10-27:** `CommitText` dispatches a cancelable trusted `beforeinput` (`insertFromComposition` while composing, otherwise `insertText`) before changing input/textarea value, then a trusted non-cancelable `input` after the value change; a canceled `beforeinput` suppresses the edit and ends composition with empty data. Explicit focus changes and blur dispatch trusted empty `compositionend` before the old host's `blur`; successful commits dispatch `compositionend` with the committed data after `input`. Regressions cover both control types, `inputType`, data, trust, `isComposing`, cancelability, value mutation, prevented commits, and compositionend-before-blur order. Making `beforeinput` non-cancelable causes the prevention regression to fail. Focused engine tests pass. Full IME update semantics remain incomplete: noncollapsed and multi-node contenteditable selections, detach-driven focus cleanup, broader WPT ordering, and composition underline/candidate rendering remain unverified.

**Live input/textarea composition updates checked 2026-10-27:** each nonempty text-control mark now replaces the previous marked passage in the actual input value or textarea text node. The sequence dispatches trusted `compositionupdate`, non-cancelable trusted `beforeinput`/`input` with `inputType="insertCompositionText"`, and changes the value between the input events; commit replaces the marked passage with final text. Empty-mark cancellation and explicit focus loss remove the marked passage and end composition. The UI reflows textarea content and paints live values without adding a duplicate overlay. Engine regressions verify the value seen by event listeners before and after each update, replacement rather than append, cancellation, blur, and final commit for both control types. Removing the live-value replacement made the event-observed DOM sequence regression fail; restoring it passed. The [Input Events Level 2 composition update order](https://www.w3.org/TR/input-events-2/#input-event-order-during-composition) defines this sequence. Focused engine/paint tests pass. Contenteditable updates now follow the same event sequence when a collapsed Text caret is available; noncollapsed and multi-node selections, maxlength interactions, detached-focus cleanup, broader WPT ordering, and composition UI decoration still need verification.

**Text-control composition paint comparison checked 2026-10-27:** Goosie headless and Chromium 131 rendered the same input and textarea values (`aかなb`) at 400×100. Pixel agreement was 97.77% at a per-channel threshold of 30 (mean absolute channel error 3.577); the visible difference was primarily Chromium's textarea resize handle. The screenshots use the final field values because the headless CLI cannot synthesize a live IME session. Engine event tests separately verify the actual update/commit sequence and paint tests verify the live candidate is shown once.

**Contenteditable live composition updates checked 2026-10-27:** when the focused host has a collapsed Selection in a Text node, each mark replaces the previous composition passage in that Text node and moves the Selection to its end. The engine emits trusted `compositionupdate`, non-cancelable trusted `beforeinput`/`input` pairs (`insertCompositionText`) around each update; commit replaces the live passage with final text and emits `insertFromComposition`, while cancel/explicit blur removes the passage before `compositionend`. Trusted `compositionstart` is cancelable; a regression confirms canceling it prevents composition from starting. A separate regression verifies that canceling commit `beforeinput` removes the active passage. Paint now reads the mutated Text node instead of splicing a duplicate overlay. Regressions verify the DOM before commit, update replacement at a nonterminal caret, values observed around each event, final commit, cancellation, and event trust/order; suppressing live Text-node replacement makes the DOM assertion fail. Selection cases without one collapsed Text-node range and detached-node focus cleanup remain incomplete; WPT composition ordering, target ranges, and composition decoration are not yet measured.

**Input live/default values checked 2026-10-28:** inputs in value-mode states now keep the live `value` separate from the `value` content attribute and `defaultValue`. Value-property writes set the input dirty-value flag and apply text/email/URL, number, date/month/week/time/local-date-time, and range sanitization; classic opaque six-digit color values normalize to lowercase hex and invalid/empty values become black. Range values default to the range midpoint, clamp to bounds, and round to the nearest step with ties toward positive infinity. Attribute changes update only a clean live value, while dirty values survive `defaultValue` and `setAttribute()` writes. Form reset restores the content-attribute value and clears dirtiness. Engine edits, rendering, accessibility, constraint validation, form submission, and `:placeholder-shown` matching now read the live value; the interactive style snapshot tracks placeholder visibility so pseudo-class updates remain incremental. Checkbox/radio default-mode values retain the attribute and `"on"` fallback behavior. Regressions cover clean attribute synchronization, dirty-value preservation, text/number/date/range/color sanitation, live-value placeholder matching and style refresh, engine edit/default separation, form submission using the live value, and reset. A mutation that disabled dirty marking made the value/default regression fail. Chromium 131 headless fixtures `forms/06-live-input-value.html` and `forms/07-range-value-sanitization.html` pass at 99.584% and 99.37% pixel agreement (per-channel tolerance 30); the visible inline-block range fixture compares the basic track, filled segment, and thumb as well as the displayed sanitized value. A focused paint regression confirms the thumb moves with the live value. Range pointer clicks and drags map horizontal position into the bounded stepped value and dispatch trusted `input` events as the value changes, followed by trusted `click` and `change` on release over the control; Arrow keys, PageUp/PageDown, and Home/End adjust the focused value and dispatch trusted input events. Regressions cover click and key cancellation, live drag values, and trusted event ordering; removing the engine-side step snap makes the browser interaction regression fail on the fractional pointer value. `go test ./test/engine ./cmd/goosie ./test/paint ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet`, and `git diff --check` pass after the drag implementation. The current 95-fixture corpus reports 91 passing and 4 failing (95.87% mean agreement). This supersedes the earlier measured write-through behavior documented by TASK-052/BR-243 for the purposes of TASK-055. fuller range-control UA styling and orientation variants, color() / alpha / Display-P3 color serialization, WPT coverage remains open.
**Input type value-mode transitions checked 2026-10-28:** changing `input.type` now applies HTML's value-mode transition steps at the DOM attribute layer, so reflected `.type`, `setAttribute()`, namespaced attribute writes, `Attr` writes, and type-attribute removal share the same state changes. A dirty, nonempty value-mode value is copied to the `value` content attribute when switching to a default/default-on mode; an empty live value leaves the default attribute untouched. Switching from a non-value mode back to a value mode restores the attribute value and clears the dirty-value flag before the new type's sanitizer runs. Entering filename mode empties the control value and clears the runtime's selected-file list. Regressions cover nonempty and empty transitions, returning to text mode, attribute removal, setAttributeNode(), default updates after returning to a clean value mode, and file-list clearing when entering filename mode. A Chromium 131 probe returned `live|live|live / after|after / original|original` for the same transition sequences. Inverting the nonempty transition guard made the regression fail on the copied value and the empty-value preservation cases; restoring it passes. `go test ./test/dom ./test/js ./test/engine ./internal/dom ./internal/js ./internal/engine -count=1`, scoped `go vet`, and `git diff --check` pass. `go test ./...` passes every package except the pre-existing `test/qc` traceability sign-off/golden check (59 automation cases await sign-off). The HTML algorithm is documented in the [input element section](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/input.html#the-input-element). Broader input-type transition coverage and WPT validation remain open.
**Text-control selection APIs checked 2026-10-28:** `HTMLInputElement` and `HTMLTextAreaElement` now expose `selectionStart`, `selectionEnd`, `selectionDirection`, `select()`, `setSelectionRange()`, and `setRangeText()`. Selection offsets use UTF-16 code units; unsupported input types return null for selection getters and throw `InvalidStateError` from selection setters/methods, while missing or invalid type keywords use the Text state. Range replacement implements select/start/end/preserve modes, and value assignments move the caret to the end. Focused JS regressions cover astral-character offsets, replacement modes, clamping/collapse, invalid type fallback, unsupported controls, and textarea API-value newline normalization; `go test ./internal/js ./test/js -count=1` passes. Selection changes queue trusted bubbling `select` events as user-interaction tasks; regressions verify deferred dispatch, payload state, and suppression when the selection is unchanged. Refreshed host-side value changes clamp stored offsets to the current UTF-16 length; mutation callbacks also clamp tracked selections after textarea child-text and CharacterData changes. Pointer clicks on single-line text inputs now place the collapsed caret at the glyph boundary nearest the click, including clicks on an already-focused input; programmatic focus and textarea focus synchronize their selection state. An engine regression checks the script-visible UTF-16 offsets at the input's left and right content edges, and `go test ./test/engine -count=1` passes. Pointer selection highlighting, drag selection, textarea line/scroll hit mapping, and a Chromium screenshot comparison of the moved caret remain unverified. These APIs follow the [HTML text-control selection algorithms](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-control-infrastructure.html#textFieldSelection); HTML conformance remains open.
**Engine text-control selection integration checked 2026-10-28:** command+A and Shift+Arrow/Home/End now establish or extend selections in focused text inputs and textareas, and the Goosie key path forwards Shift state to the engine. Typing, Backspace/Delete, and paste replace the selected range; the runtime selection properties follow the engine caret/anchor in UTF-16 code units, and copy reads the selected substring. Focused engine and browser-shell regressions cover select-all replacement, Shift+Left selection direction, replacement, and script-visible offsets. `go test ./test/engine ./cmd/goosie ./internal/js -count=1`, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` pass. Full `go test ./...` passes all packages except the existing `test/qc` traceability sign-off check (59 automation cases await sign-off). Pointer-based caret/drag selection, visible selection highlighting, and modifier selection in contenteditable remain open.
**Textarea IDL attributes checked 2026-10-28:** `HTMLTextAreaElement` now exposes readonly `type` (`"textarea"`) and `textLength` (UTF-16 length of the newline-normalized API value), plus reflected `cols`, `rows`, and `wrap`. `cols` and `rows` use defaults 20 and 2, parse positive values up to 2,147,483,647, and apply the Web IDL unsigned-long conversion and positive-with-fallback setter algorithm; `wrap` reflects the content attribute as a DOMString. Focused JS regressions cover defaults, valid/invalid attributes, plus-sign integer parsing, range overflow, setter conversion, raw wrap reflection, readonly type, and astral-character length. A Chromium headless probe confirms default values, plus-sign parsing, and `cols = -1` fallback/attribute rewriting. `go test ./test/js -run TestTextareaSpecificIDLAttributes -count=1` passes; the pinned WPT checkout is absent from this worktree, so no upstream textarea IDL WPT was run. Visual sizing from `rows`/`cols`, browser-aligned hard-wrap line breaks, and broader textarea API/WPT coverage remain open; this is not textarea or HTML conformance closure.

**Textarea hard-wrap submission checked 2026-10-28:** `wrap="hard"` now applies a bounded submission-value transformation without changing `textarea.value`: it inserts LF at the `cols` boundary while preserving authored line breaks. `FormData(form)` and Goosie's engine-side form collection use that transformed value. URL-encoded, multipart, and text/plain submission paths normalize line endings to CRLF at encoding time. JS and engine regressions verify wrapped entries, unchanged API values, line-boundary behavior, and CRLF URL encoding; forcing the DOM helper to skip hard wrapping makes both regressions fail. The selected algorithm wraps at no more than `cols` Unicode scalar values per line, an implementation-defined choice allowed by the [HTML textarea wrapping transformation](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-elements.html#textarea-wrapping-transformation). A Chromium probe with `cols=4` wrapped at six characters for its rendered control width, so exact browser line breaks remain unaligned; textarea layout sizing and visual line-wrap integration are still open. `go test ./internal/dom ./internal/engine ./internal/js ./test/engine ./test/js -count=1`, scoped vet, architecture checks, and `git diff --check` pass. The pinned WPT checkout is absent, so no upstream form-submission WPT was run.

**Textarea visual dimensions checked 2026-10-28:** the UA rule now gives textarea monospace at 13.333 CSS px and `pre-wrap`; `wrap="off"` uses `pre`. Intrinsic content width uses `cols` multiplied by the font's zero-glyph advance, and intrinsic height uses `rows` multiplied by line-height. A layout regression verifies the defaults are wider than `cols=10`, `rows=4` is taller than `rows=2`, and computed font is monospace. The inline-block row sizing path preserves replaced-control intrinsic dimensions rather than shrinking the textarea to its content. `go test ./internal/dom ./internal/layout ./internal/style ./internal/engine ./internal/js ./test/engine ./test/js -count=1` passes. Full headless parity across 96 fixtures reports 92 passing, 4 failing, and 95.91% mean agreement; `forms/08-textarea-live-value.png` passes. The existing four failing fixtures are `selectors/02-pseudo-classes`, `complex/01-dashboard`, `complex/04-login-form`, and `semantic/03-aside-figure`. This closes visual rows/cols sizing only; wrapping still uses `cols` in submission transformation, while Chromium observations show line breaks follow rendered content width. Broader textarea API and WPT coverage remain open.

**Output element value and reset algorithms checked 2026-10-28:** `HTMLOutputElement.type`, `name`, `form`, `htmlFor` (`DOMTokenList`), `labels`, `value`, `defaultValue`, and constraint-validation surface are now exposed. The default-value override follows HTML: before an override, setting `defaultValue` replaces descendant text; setting `value` snapshots the current default, replaces descendant text, and subsequent `defaultValue` writes update the override without changing current text. `form.reset()` dispatches a trusted, non-bubbling, cancelable `reset` event, honors cancellation, resets input/textarea/output values, clears checkbox/radio dirty checkedness, and clears file selections; reset-button `.click()` uses the same reset path. The engine reset path also restores output text and its default-value override and checkbox/radio checkedness. Output stays listed in `form.elements` but is omitted from `FormData`; its `willValidate` is false, and `checkValidity()` succeeds even with a custom error. Focused JS and engine regressions cover those rules, stable `htmlFor`, labels, event trust/cancellation, reset-button activation, and reset from the engine. A mutation that disabled default-value override tracking failed the output value, canceled-reset default, and reset-button regressions; restoring the flag passed `go test ./internal/dom ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/engine -count=1`. A 400×120 headless render of a script-updated output was 99.33% pixel agreement with Chromium at per-channel tolerance 30 (mean absolute channel error 0.712). Repository-wide tests pass all packages except `test/qc`, which remains gated on QC sign-off for 59 cases and its protected stale traceability golden. Custom-element reset callbacks and upstream WPT coverage remain open; this is not form or HTML conformance closure.

**Input checkedness, form-control cloning, and parser radio groups checked 2026-10-28:** input checkedness is separate from the `checked` content attribute. `checked` reads/writes the live state and marks it dirty; `defaultChecked` remains reflected from the attribute, and attribute mutations synchronize current checkedness only while clean. The checked state feeds engine activation, radio-group script assignment, `:checked`, required validation, successful form submission, and checkbox/radio paint. Form reset clears dirty checkedness, restores the attribute default, and preserves radio-group exclusivity in both the JS and engine reset paths. HTML input cloning copies current value, dirty value, checkedness, and dirty checkedness; textarea cloning copies its raw value and dirty-value flag. Parser insertion now enforces exclusivity for connected checked radios using nonempty name and form owner as specified by the [radio button group algorithm](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/input.html#radio-button-state-(type=radio)); radios in separate forms or with empty names remain independent, and arbitration leaves each `checked` content attribute intact. Go regressions cover attribute/default/live separation, dirty attribute synchronization, script radio exclusivity, parser insertion groups, reset, cloned live state, and the command-line checkbox click path. Disabling insertion arbitration made the parser regression fail; mutating the live checked getter to read the attribute made the checked/default, script radio group, and clone tests fail. Focused DOM/CSS/engine/JS/paint suites, `go test ./cmd/goosie -count=1`, architecture checks, and `go vet -all ./...` pass. A 400×100 headless probe of two checked same-name radios produced `[false, true]` in Chromium and rendered the same selected control in Goosie at 97.43% full-frame pixel agreement at tolerance 30 (mean absolute channel error 3.869). The serialized full repository run passes all packages except the protected `test/qc` traceability gate; the QC golden was left untouched. The pinned WPT checkout is absent, so no upstream input checkedness test was run. See the dynamic-radio and select/option checkpoints below; the broader select API remains open.

**Dynamic radio groups and reset exclusivity checked 2026-10-28:** connected radio groups now reconcile after checked/defaultChecked, name, type, and form-attribute mutations, form ID changes, and script-driven node insertion, movement, removal, replacement, `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, text replacement, and shadow-root mutations. Parser-assigned form-owner pointers are cleared on script tree mutations so a moved radio resolves its current owner from its explicit `form` attribute or current ancestors. Detached form-owner groups reconcile on insertion; insertion into a detached tree without a form owner preserves the checkedness of already-checked radios, while a later checked IDL write still enforces exclusivity within that tree scope. The detached root continues to define required-radio validity grouping; empty names remain ungrouped. Automatically unchecked peers become dirty, matching Chromium's behavior when later checked-attribute changes must leave their live state unchanged. JS and engine form reset process radio defaults while preserving group exclusivity. Regressions cover name/type/form changes, detached checked setters and insertion, `innerHTML` replacing existing checked radios, form ID resolution, shadow trees, moved parser-owned radios, automatic-peer dirtiness, and reset in both JS and engine paths. Disabling dirty-group reconciliation made the name/type/form, connection, shadow, form-ID, and move tests fail; the parser insertion mutation also fails its dedicated DOM regression. The dynamic name-change headless probe matches Chromium's `[false, true]` live state and renders at 97.43% full-frame pixel agreement at tolerance 30 (mean absolute channel error 3.869). The pinned `radio-disconnected-group-owner.html` passes 8/8 tests in the current focused WPT selection. Focused DOM/JS/engine suites, architecture checks, and vet pass. The serialized repository test run passes except for the protected QC traceability gate (59 automated cases await sign-off and the golden is stale); the golden remains unchanged. DOM adoption through all range/editing algorithms and reset/association behavior for every form-associated control remain broader open areas; this does not close HTML conformance.

**Select/option selectedness, collections, multi-select submission, and reset checked 2026-10-28:** option live selectedness and dirtiness are stored separately from the `selected` content attribute. `option.selected` writes current state, single-select selection clears peers without rewriting defaults, and clean `selected` attribute mutations synchronize live state and reapply single-select arbitration. Select `.value`/`.selectedIndex`, `.length`, `.type`, `.multiple`, `.size`, `.disabled`, `.required`, and `.name` are exposed; `options` is a stable live `HTMLOptionsCollection`, and `selectedOptions` is a stable live `HTMLCollection`. Both collections support indexed reads, `item()`, and `namedItem()`; the options collection also supports indexed replacement, a bounded writable `length`, `add()`, and `remove()`, with matching select-level `add()`/`remove()` methods. Options expose `selected`, `defaultSelected`, `disabled`, `form`, `index`, `label`, `text`, and `value`; `option.label` distinguishes an absent label attribute (normalized option text) from a present empty attribute (empty string). Central DOM insertion/removal normalizes affected single-selects, including options inserted under an optgroup. Removing `multiple` keeps the first current selection, and size transitions reapply the default-selection algorithm; disabled options inside disabled optgroups are skipped by defaulting and keyboard navigation. The first enabled option is selected by default for display-size-one selects, while an explicitly cleared or unmatched selection stays empty. CSS `:checked`, select painting, accessibility, keyboard movement, and form submission consume live selectedness. JS `FormData(form)` and engine form submission now emit one same-name entry for each selected, enabled option in tree order; disabled options and disabled-optgroup descendants are omitted. Chromium returns `color=red|color=Blue Sky` for the focused selected/disabled fixture, matching both new JS and engine regressions. Both JS `form.reset()` and engine reset restore selectedness from option attributes and clear dirtiness. Cloning preserves option live/dirty state and an intentionally empty select. Regressions cover live/default separation, dirty attribute changes, collection identity/liveness/indexing, single and multiple selection, option IDL, value/index/length setters, collection and select add/remove, multiple/size changes, keyboard movement, multi-entry submission, and both reset routes. Goosie headless showed a script-inserted selected option in the control label as Chromium did; the 240×80 pixel agreement was 91.36% at channel tolerance 30 (mean error 14.09), with most mismatch from Chromium's native select border. The work follows the [WHATWG option selectedness and dirtiness algorithms](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-elements.html#the-option-element), [select collection and value/index algorithms](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-elements.html#the-select-element), and [form entry-list construction](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-control-infrastructure.html#constructing-the-entry-list). Remaining gaps include full WebIDL/IDL reflection edge cases, collection named-property semantics, select/option attribute mutation edge cases, selection notification timing, optgroup UI interaction, and pinned WPT coverage; this does not complete select or HTML conformance.

**Required select constraint validation checked 2026-10-28:** `HTMLSelectElement` now exposes the shared `ValidityState`, `willValidate`, `validationMessage`, `checkValidity()`, `reportValidity()`, and `setCustomValidity()` surface. `valueMissing` follows the required-select rules: no selected option is missing; for a required single-line single-select, selecting its empty-valued first option is missing only when that option is not inside an optgroup; selecting another empty-valued option is valid; and a required multiple select is valid with any selected option, including an empty-valued one. Engine-side form submission validation and JS control/form validity use the same DOM predicate. JS tests assert validity flags, methods, and `:valid`/`:invalid`; engine regressions cover placeholder, non-placeholder empty value, multiple selection, optgroup ancestry, and no selection. This closes required-select missing-value behavior only; other select validity details, exhaustive WebIDL behavior, selection notification timing, optgroup UI interaction, and pinned WPT coverage remain open. The rules follow the [WHATWG select constraint-validation algorithm](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-elements.html#the-select-element).

**Disabled fieldset first-legend behavior checked 2026-10-28:** a shared DOM predicate now applies disabled fieldset state to descendant disableable elements while exempting descendants of each fieldset's first HTML `legend` child. CSS `:disabled`, engine and JS form validation/data collection, focusability, keyboard activation, `click()` and label activation, select interaction, and select accessibility state use this rule. Nested disabled fieldsets are evaluated independently, so an inner first-legend exception does not bypass a disabled outer fieldset. JS coverage verifies `willValidate`, `:disabled`, blocked direct/label clicks, and `FormData`; engine coverage verifies only eligible legend controls are submitted. Focused DOM/CSS/engine/JS packages, architecture checks, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` pass. This closes native disabled-fieldset propagation for these paths only; form-associated custom-element edge cases, dynamic edge-case WPT coverage, and full HTML conformance remain open. The first-legend rule is specified by the [WHATWG fieldset element](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-elements.html#the-fieldset-element).

**Fieldset interface, validation, and rendering-update focus fixup checked 2026-10-28:** `HTMLFieldSetElement` now exposes constant `type === "fieldset"`, a cached live `elements` collection, and the shared constraint-validation methods/properties. When rendering is pumped after a focused control becomes disabled through a fieldset, focus is blurred per the disabled-control fixup path. Dynamically inserted IDs install live Window named-property accessors, including IDs parsed through `innerHTML`. The per-call fragment parser depth cap for `innerHTML` and `insertAdjacentHTML` is aligned to the engine's depth limit of 128, allowing bounded deeply nested content; the pinned 100-level fieldset focus case then completes without hanging. The newly curated `html/semantics/forms/the-fieldset-element` directory passes 10/10 runnable files against pinned WPT commit `11ae8c1179be2dd38070846b01c2e9338ef81770` (0 failed, skipped, or errored), covering interface type/elements, nested disabledness, first-legend exceptions, validation, and deep focus fixup. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/engine ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` pass. This is one focused directory and does not imply broad HTML conformance; TASK-055 remains incomplete.

**Form requestSubmit navigation timing checked 2026-10-28:** same-origin form GET navigation to an iframe is now scheduled as a task, allowing code after `requestSubmit()` to install the target's `load` handler before navigation completes. A later same-frame navigation replaces the earlier pending destination across GET and POST submissions. Iframe `Location` now stringifies to its live URL, so comparisons such as `location == "about:blank"` and URL construction observe the platform URL value. The selected pinned WPT files `request-submit-activation.html`, `form-double-submit-requestsubmit.html`, and `submit-event.html` pass 3/3 (0 failed, skipped, or errored) at commit `11ae8c1179be2dd38070846b01c2e9338ef81770`; the existing external-iframe form-navigation regression now advances the child event loop before checking its result. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/engine -count=1`, `go vet -all ./...`, the architecture test, and `git diff --check` pass. This does not measure the full form-submission directory or general navigation semantics; TASK-055 remains incomplete.

**Form entry-list reentrancy and planned-navigation replacement checked 2026-10-28:** form submission and `FormData(form)` now share a per-form entry-list construction guard. A `formdata` handler that calls `form.submit()` no longer recursively constructs another entry list or fires another `formdata` event; submission also aborts if the form is removed during entry-list construction. Planned navigation state is tracked by source form, so a later default-action submission replaces an earlier `form.submit()` navigation even when it targets a different iframe. Empty GET submissions preserve the required non-null query delimiter. A focused Go regression verifies nested `formdata`/`submit` behavior. At this checkpoint, `form-submission-algorithm.html` passed all 11 subtests; the five-file selection had 4 passes and one timeout in `form-data-set-usv.html`. The following follow-up resolves that timeout. This does not complete the form-submission suite; TASK-055 remains incomplete.

**Iframe-owned form navigation and Unicode POST echo checked 2026-10-28:** when parent-realm script invokes a form method on a child document, navigation now resolves the child document's owning iframe and targets that browsing context. The WPT server implements the pinned `form-echo.py` text/plain endpoint, returning lowercase, space-separated hexadecimal bytes with a bounded request body. The combined eight-file selection (`form-submission-algorithm.html`, `form-submit-invalid.html`, `form-submitters.html`, `form-double-submit.html`, `form-data-set-usv.html`, `request-submit-activation.html`, `form-double-submit-requestsubmit.html`, and `submit-event.html`) passes 8/8 against pinned commit `11ae8c1179be2dd38070846b01c2e9338ef81770`, with 0 failed, skipped, or errored files. This selection includes the previously timing-out Unicode text/plain POST. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/engine ./test/wpt ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` pass. The full form-submission directory and overall HTML conformance remain open.

**Iframe Location and data-URL form targets checked 2026-10-28:** child-document `Document.location` now returns the containing iframe's stable Location object; assigning it forwards to `href` and schedules a URL-resolved iframe navigation that supersedes pending form navigation. Iframe Location exposes URL parts including `pathname`, `protocol`, `host`, `hostname`, `port`, `hash`, and `origin`. HTML URL parsing removes ASCII tab/newline characters from inputs before parsing, including multiline `data:` form actions. On pinned WPT, the selected form-submission/navigation set now passes 13/13 files, adding `getactionurl.html`, `form-submit-action.html`, `form-double-submit-default-action.html`, `reparent-form-during-planned-navigation-task.html`, and `form-submit-iframe-then-location-navigate.html` to the prior eight-file set. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/engine ./test/wpt ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` pass. This remains a selected set from `form-submission-0`; full HTML Living Standard conformance is incomplete.

**Full pinned `form-submission-0` directory closed, 2026-10-28:** form navigation now schedules per browsing context and avoids the initial `about:blank` load winning over a planned GET; default-click submission cancels earlier click-handler navigations only after the default `submit` event is accepted. Reentrant `form.submit()` still snapshots data during submit handlers. Form data now uses canonical document/`accept-charset` encodings for `_charset_` and action queries, implicit Enter submission is modeled in the WPT input shim, and `javascript:` anchor execution uses the owning iframe realm. Cross-origin `contentDocument` is null; the WPT server maps reachable loopback aliases, expands remote-origin/port placeholders, and handles the bounded file-upload endpoint used by the pinned suite. The complete pinned `html/semantics/forms/form-submission-0` directory passes 31/31 files (0 failed, skipped, or errored), including all form-data, requestSubmit, multi-target, cross-origin, JavaScript-URL, file-upload, and SubmitEvent cases. This closes only that audited directory; other HTML Living Standard directories and overall TASK-055 acceptance remain open.

**Textarea live/default values checked 2026-10-28:** `textarea.value` now has live value state separate from descendant text, which remains `defaultValue`/`textContent`. Script writes strip carriage returns and dirty the value; clean child-text and `defaultValue` changes synchronize the live value, while dirty values survive those changes. Engine typing and composition edit the live value, layout displays that value without modifying the DOM text, form data submits it, and form reset restores descendant text and clears dirtiness. Placeholder matching, required/maxlength validity, accessibility values, and directionality read the live value. Go regressions cover property reflection, line-ending normalization, clean/dirty synchronization, CharacterData mutation, live rendering, form serialization, reset, and preservation of default text during editing. The pinned jQuery support probe now reports `noCloneChecked=true` because `defaultValue` is reflected. Removing the live textarea value getter made the rendering regression paint `default` instead of `live text`. Chromium 131 headless fixture `forms/08-textarea-live-value.html` passes at 99.51% pixel agreement (per-channel tolerance 30); the refreshed 96-fixture corpus reports 92 passing, 4 failing, 95.91% mean agreement, with the same four existing failures (`selectors/02-pseudo-classes`, `complex/01-dashboard`, `complex/04-login-form`, and `semantic/03-aside-figure`). `go test ./test/css ./test/js ./test/dom ./test/engine ./test/layout ./cmd/goosie ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet`, and `git diff --check` pass. `go test ./...` passes every package except `test/qc`, whose traceability golden remains stale pending QC sign-off for 59 automated cases; §8 still forbids updating that golden without an explicit proposal. Textarea API edge cases and WPT coverage, fuller range-control UA styling/orientation, broader color serialization, additional type-change transitions, and the overall HTML Living Standard acceptance criteria remain open.

**Text-control length limits checked 2026-10-27:** text-control user-edit paths now measure values and insertions in UTF-16 code units, matching HTML's `maxlength`/`minlength` length definition; `maxlength="0"` is enforced instead of being treated as unlimited. The shared parser follows HTML's non-negative-integer rules, including leading ASCII whitespace, an optional sign, trailing characters after the digit run, and overflow saturation for values larger than the platform integer range. Input and textarea expose `maxLength`/`minLength` reflection, including the `-1` getter fallback and `IndexSizeError` for negative assignments. Ordinary insertion truncates only at scalar-value boundaries, and keyboard insertion, accessibility replacement, and composition commit all use the same code-unit count. Regressions cover supplementary-plane characters, truncation without splitting a surrogate pair, reflected properties, `SetFocusedText`, zero-length limits, forgiving attribute parsing, overflow, and composition commit rejection. Intermediate IME preview remains allowed to exceed the maximum; the committed control value is checked against it. HTML permits user agents to prevent an over-limit API value. Broader textarea API edge cases, native validity, and WPT coverage remain open.

**Native text-control validity checked 2026-10-28:** `ValidityState` objects are cached per input/textarea, expose dynamic `patternMismatch`, `rangeOverflow`, `rangeUnderflow`, `stepMismatch`, `tooLong`, `tooShort`, `typeMismatch`, `valueMissing`, `customError`, and `valid` flags, and identify as `ValidityState`. User-edited values outside `maxlength`/`minlength` trigger native `checkValidity()` and form `checkValidity()` failure with `invalid` dispatch and match `:invalid`; empty values do not trigger `tooShort`, and script-set values do not trigger either length flag. Textarea length measurement concatenates text content, normalizes CRLF and CR to one LF, then counts UTF-16 units. Native `setCustomValidity()` stores a newline-normalized message, updates `customError`/`valid`/`validationMessage`, blocks control and form validity checks, and clears when passed an empty string. Disabled controls retain `customError` but are excluded from validation and return an empty validation message. Input patterns are checked as anchored Unicode regexes for nonempty values; invalid patterns are ignored. Email controls check one address or each trimmed member of a `multiple` list, and URL controls check for an absolute URL; empty values remain exempt from `typeMismatch`. These flags participate in control/form `checkValidity()`, validation messages, and cached CSS `:invalid` matching after JS or engine edits. Number, date, month, week, time, and local date-time inputs calculate range underflow/overflow from valid `min`/`max` values and step mismatch from a positive step (invalid/nonpositive values fall back to the state default), `any`, and the HTML step base (valid `min`, then the `value` content attribute, then the state default base). Date strings use validated proleptic-Gregorian day numbers; month, ISO week, time, and local date-time values use their state-specific numeric units and step scales. Reversed time ranges follow the periodic-domain rule. Number quotients use a small floating-point tolerance. `:in-range` and `:out-of-range` reflect these numeric and temporal range limits. Email validity uses the standard's ASCII-compatible grammar after Unicode domains are mapped to ASCII with the IDNA lookup profile; URL checking uses Go's `net/url`, so full WHATWG URL parser behavior remains open; `badInput`, textarea live/default state, color() / alpha / Display-P3 color serialization, and complete type-change transitions remain incomplete. Range and basic opaque color value sanitization are implemented with focused JS regressions as recorded above; range rendering now paints a basic horizontal track, active segment, and thumb from the live value. Horizontal click positioning and Arrow/Home/End/PageUp/PageDown value changes have focused event regressions; pointer dragging, vertical writing modes, and full native styling remain open. The matcher uses Goja's ECMAScript-to-RE2 transformer, so the implemented subset uses a linear-time matcher; engine edits and JS value/attribute changes refresh the cached CSS validity flag. UnicodeSets `v` set operators, lookarounds/backreferences, and full flag refresh for every DOM mutation path remain open. Regressions cover object identity/prototype, script versus user changes, form-level checks, invalid events, CSS pseudo-class matching after JS and engine edits, supplementary characters, empty values, IDL reflection, textarea newline counting across multiple Text nodes, custom-message normalization/clearing, disabled-control behavior, matching/mismatching patterns, trimmed comma-separated `multiple=email` pattern members, invalid-pattern handling, valid/invalid email and URL values, empty-value exemptions, number underflow/overflow/step cases, `step=any`, decimal steps, date leap-day/boundary/step cases, month/week steps, reversed time ranges, local date-time steps, and range pseudo-classes. Native constraint validity remains partial: `badInput`, textarea value state, localized native messages, and WPT coverage are unimplemented or unverified. At the time this validity checkpoint was recorded, the pinned checkout was absent; subsequent numeric and date-time WPT results are recorded in the input entries above.

**Contenteditable composition paint comparison checked 2026-10-27:** Goosie headless and Chromium 131 rendered the same `contenteditable` text (`aかなb`) at 400×80. Pixel agreement was 99.14% at a per-channel threshold of 30 (mean absolute channel error 1.188). The screenshots compare the final text because headless cannot synthesize an in-progress IME session; engine tests separately verify the live Text-node changes and event order.

**IME preview visual comparison checked 2026-10-27:** rendered identical `aXb` and `aかb` lines at 400×120 with Goosie headless and Chromium 131. The ASCII line matched 99.79% of pixels at parity's per-channel tolerance of 30 (mean absolute channel error 0.211); the Japanese line matched 99.42% (mean absolute channel error 0.897). The initial Japanese render showed a missing-glyph box; the bounded TTC fallback change below now draws kana. These screenshots compare the final display-list text with Chromium's equivalent text because the headless CLI cannot synthesize an in-progress IME event. Engine regressions verify live text insertion at the Selection offset. The pixel comparisons validate glyph placement, not composition underline, candidate UI, or the full IME event lifecycle.

**Bounded macOS CJK symbol-font fallback checked 2026-10-27:** the rasterizer's lazy fallback path now reads cmap coverage across TTC face directories and selects a face from a collection that covers the missing rune. It admits `/System/Library/Fonts/CJKSymbolsFallback.ttc` only when needed; the local collection is 790,260 bytes, below the existing 4 MiB per-font cap, and includes Hiragana `か`. A host-conditional raster regression verifies the kana glyph is non-tofu and that a bounded fallback face was retained; removing this candidate makes the test fail with a tofu glyph. The Goosie/Chromium `aかb` screenshot comparison above improved from a tofu glyph to 99.42% pixel agreement; this does not add Kanji coverage or complete complex-script shaping. Broader font fallback and shaping remain open.

**DOM Range and XML document-construction checkpoint checked 2026-10-27:** Range comparison follows the four standard boundary modes, rejects invalid modes with `NotSupportedError` and cross-root comparisons with `WrongDocumentError`, and validates point offsets as unsigned Web IDL values against UTF-16 node lengths. `comparePoint()` and `isPointInRange()` validate roots, offsets, and doctype nodes; the Range constructor and constants are exposed, and detached XML documents receive `createRange()`. DOM nodes expose `compareDocumentPosition()` with tree-order, ancestor, and disconnected-root flags. The WPT fixture setup also required `Document.createCDATASection()`, `createProcessingInstruction()`, and `DOMImplementation.createDocument()`; these bindings are now present. Pinned WPT `Range-compareBoundaryPoints.html`, `Range-comparePoint.html`, and `Range-isPointInRange.html` pass 3/3. The iframe WindowProxy bridge now propagates parent writes into the child runtime, synchronizes child globals after exported functions run, forwards live Range objects, and preserves DOMException identity across realms. Range content algorithms now capture original node containment and CharacterData slice offsets before mutations, preventing shifted siblings from being treated as newly selected. On the pinned WPT commit, `Range-deleteContents.html` passes all 125 subtests, and `Range-cloneContents.html` and `Range-extractContents.html` pass all 187 subtests each, including cross-node boundaries and XML document/comment boundaries. Zero-length endpoint Text and Comment nodes are retained in the returned fragments. DOM name construction now uses the DOM Standard's broader valid-element-name and namespace-prefix rules rather than XML's stricter QName production. Undefined namespace converts to null; undefined `qualifiedName` converts to `"undefined"`. The pinned `DOMImplementation-createDocument.html` and `Document-createElementNS.html` pass (5/5 selected DOM and Range comparison WPT files overall); focused Go regressions cover DOM name rules, partial clone/extract tree shapes, moved node identity, detached character data, and boundary behavior. These checks do not establish complete DOM Range or HTML conformance.

**CharacterData methods checked 2026-10-27:** `substringData()`, `appendData()`, `insertData()`, `deleteData()`, and `replaceData()` are exposed on the shared CharacterData prototype, use UTF-16 offsets, apply unsigned-long wrapping, throw `IndexSizeError` for offsets beyond node length, and require their specified arguments. The `data` setter converts null to the empty string, `ChildNode.remove()` works for Text, Comment, and ProcessingInstruction, child insertion on CharacterData throws `HierarchyRequestError`, and `childNodes` exposes supported own indexed properties. Go regressions cover non-BMP offsets, method sequencing, and isolated-surrogate round trips through `data`, `nodeValue`, `textContent`, `substringData()`, and `cloneNode()`. CharacterData stores otherwise-unrepresentable UTF-16 code units separately from its Go string view so JS accessors and mutators preserve isolated surrogates. The pinned nine-file CharacterData WPT selection passes 9/9, including all eight isolated-surrogate cases. This selection does not establish full CharacterData or HTML conformance.

**XML parsing/serialization follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** XML parsing now retains CDATA as DOM node type 4 (`CDATASection`), exposes its `nodeName`, `nodeValue`, and `textContent`, and serializes each section with its original delimiters. The XML document binding also exposes `charset`, `inputEncoding`, `contentType`, and `location`; parsed XML elements without a namespace now correctly report `namespaceURI === null`, and malformed/unterminated elements produce a namespaced `parsererror` document. Go regressions verify adjacent CDATA round-trip, metadata, and interface identity. Before the changes, pinned WPT `domparsing/xml-parse-serialize-roundtrip.html` failed both cases, while the XML DOMParser metadata file and CDATA file failed their assertions; afterward, `xml-parse-serialize-roundtrip.html`, `XMLSerializer-serializeToString.html` (33 subtests), `DOMParser-parseFromString-xml.html` (20 subtests), and `DOMParser-parseFromString-xml-CDATA.html` (8 subtests) pass. Replacing CDATA serialization with escaped text made the round-trip regression fail; restoring the implementation made it pass. `go test ./test/js -count=1`, `go test ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, and scoped `go vet -all ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/wpt` pass. The earlier broad `domparsing` run remains 8/78 passed, 55 failed, and 15 errored; this focused cluster does not complete XML or HTML conformance, and streaming-markup APIs remain gaps.

**`domparsing` suite refreshed 2026-10-27:** with the same pinned WPT commit and current headless binary, the complete curated directory run now reports 13/78 passing, 50 failing, 15 errored, 0 skipped (16.7%); the non-tentative directory is 12/43 passing and tentative is 1/35. This improves on the prior 8/78 measurement but still leaves most cases failing or errored. Failures include tentative streaming-markup methods, cross-realm DOMParser cases, styleSheets and document-write behavior, and XML readyState/API details. Treat this as a partial suite measurement, not an acceptance gate for TASK-055.

**XML readyState and comment creation follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** detached documents now report `readyState === "complete"`, and the active document exposes `createComment(data)` through its DOM factory. The focused pinned WPTs `xmldomparser.html` and `Comment-serialization-double-hyphen.html` pass 2/2; Go regressions cover detached XML metadata and comment creation/HTML serialization. Together with the XML parser focused set above, this closes these individual cases only; the broader directory remains at its measured 13/78 result until rerun.

**DOMParser stylesheet CSSOM follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** documents expose a live `styleSheets` collection; style elements yield cached `CSSStyleSheet` wrappers, and parsed CSS style rules expose `CSSRule`/`CSSStyleRule`, `selectorText`, and `cssText` through `cssRules`. Added Go coverage verifies live collection length, stylesheet wrapper identity, CSSOM interface identity, rule count, and selector text. The pinned `DOMParser-parseFromString-stylesheets.html` WPT passes all 6 tests. Returning an empty rule set made the Go regression fail on the missing selector rule; restored parsing passes. Full CSSOM mutation, imported/external sheets, media state, and the rest of stylesheet conformance remain open; the broad `domparsing` count has not been rerun after these focused fixes.

**DOMParser scripting-disabled parsing checked 2026-10-27:** `DOMParser.parseFromString(..., "text/html")` now calls the bounded HTML parser with scripting disabled, and in-body `<noscript>` only enters RAWTEXT when scripting is enabled. A Go regression verifies a parsed `<noscript><p>` contains an actual paragraph element; mutating the tree builder to always use RAWTEXT made the regression fail, then the original condition was restored. `DOMParser-parseFromString-html.html` now passes 10/10 WPT tests, and the existing parser scripting-flag regression passes. The complete `domparsing` suite has not been rerun since its last 13/78 checkpoint.

**`domparsing` suite refreshed after XML/CSSOM/DOMParser fixes, 2026-10-27:** the pinned full directory run now reports 17/78 passing, 46 failing, 15 errored, 0 skipped (21.8%). The non-tentative directory is 16/43 passing (22 failures, 5 errors); tentative remains 1/35 (24 failures, 10 errors). This is up from 13/78 at the preceding checkpoint.
