The latest actionable failures include DOMParser document URL/baseURI across iframe contexts, readyState details, and broad tentative streaming-markup API gaps. No broad HTML conformance claim follows from this directory result.

**DOMParser associated URL and iframe-navigation follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** detached parser documents now retain the URL of the DOMParser instance's associated document. Their `baseURI` resolves against that URL and `<base>` elements in the parsed document, without inheriting the caller document's `<base>`. DOMParser constructors are available on iframe Window objects; parser methods use the receiver instance's associated URL even when called through the other realm's prototype. When an iframe navigates, a parser created by the old document keeps that document's URL; same-document history changes remain reflected. The five pinned `DOMParser-parseFromString-url*.html` WPTs pass 5/5. A Go regression verifies an outer `<base>` does not leak into a parsed document; mutating the navigation check to always use the current iframe URL made the five old-document assertions fail, then the implementation was restored. Existing WPT URL files and this focused regression pass after restoration. Remaining parser gaps are recorded in the current full-directory result above; this focused URL cluster does not establish all URL, iframe, or HTML behavior.

**`domparsing` refreshed after associated-URL fix, 2026-10-27:** the current pinned full-directory run reports 22/78 passing, 41 failing, 15 errored, 0 skipped (28.2%); non-tentative cases are 21/43 passing (17 failures, 5 errors), tentative remains 1/35 (24 failures, 10 errors). This is up from 17/78 at the prior checkpoint. The five URL WPT files are now green; remaining failures include XML well-formedness/entity cases, DOM import/contextual-fragment methods, and tentative streaming markup. This broad suite remains far short of TASK-055 acceptance.

**XML well-formedness and entity follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** XML DOMParser now validates input with strict `encoding/xml` tokenization plus document-root checks and QName/namespace binding checks before constructing the Goosie DOM. It rejects malformed attributes, invalid or unbound namespace prefixes, multiple roots, malformed character references, and undeclared general entities as parsererror documents. Internal general entities declared in a doctype subset and decimal/hexadecimal character references are expanded; expansion is depth/size bounded. Go regressions cover malformed inputs and entity expansion. The pinned `DOMParser-parseFromString-xml-parsererror.html`, `DOMParser-xml-character-entities.html`, and `DOMParser-parseFromString-xml-internal-subset.html` WPTs pass 3/3. Disabling the validator caused the malformed-XML regression to fail on an unquoted attribute and a multiple-root document; restoring validation returns it to pass. This closes those cases only; XML namespaces, DTDs, entities, and XML well-formedness conformance are not complete.

**`domparsing` refreshed after XML validation, 2026-10-27:** the full pinned directory now reports 25/78 passing, 38 failing, 15 errored, 0 skipped (32.1%). Non-tentative cases are 24/43 passing (14 failures, 5 errors); tentative remains 1/35 (24 failures, 10 errors). This is up from 22/78 at the last checkpoint; the three targeted XML parsererror/entity WPT files now pass. Remaining failures include DOM `importNode` and `createContextualFragment`, XML `innerHTML`, and tentative streaming markup. Full HTML Living Standard acceptance remains unmet.

**Baseline checked 2026-10-26:** the current worktree already contains an in-progress parser/DOM/API/layout pass, and `go test ./...` passes. This verifies the Go tests currently present; it does not establish HTML conformance. `testdata/wpt-config.json` selects ten visual-test directories, so the current WPT run cannot establish full HTML support. Keep the existing in-progress changes intact while closing the remaining requirements.

**Parser progress checked 2026-10-26:** `<title>`/`<textarea>` now decode character references without decoding script data, and the scripting-enabled parser treats `<iframe>`, `<noembed>`, `<noframes>`, `<noscript>`, and `<xmp>` as raw text; `<plaintext>` consumes the remainder of input. Regression coverage passes with `go test ./test/dom ./internal/dom`. This closes only those cases: the custom tokenizer/tree builder still lacks a full upstream parser-conformance run, and the scripting flag is not configurable yet.

**Parser conformance baseline checked 2026-10-26:** the WPT curator now exposes `html/syntax/parsing`, which the earlier `WPT_DIRS` attempt silently did not run because only curated directories were discoverable. Against pinned WPT commit `11ae8c1179be2dd38070846b01c2e9338ef81770`, the directory discovers 62 runnable cases: 2 passed, 41 failed, and 19 timed out. This exposes parsing gaps in MathML/SVG foreign content, template fragment parsing and DOM bindings, character-reference edge cases, and parser lifecycle behavior; adoption-agency and script-reparenting cases time out in the current WPT execution path. HTML input preprocessing now drops an initial BOM, normalizes CRLF/CR to LF, and replaces NUL in document input. The upstream newline-normalization case passes. Go's decoder was missing the standard multi-code-point `nGt`/`nLt` names; those entries now pass the full `named-character-references.html` test. `zero.html` still fails 9/14 subtests, including fragment-parser NUL-state behavior and missing fragment DOM accessors. Unit coverage and focused WPT results do not close the parser chapter.

**Parser suite refreshed 2026-10-26:** the pinned 62-case `html/syntax/parsing` run is now 22 passed, 22 failed, 17 timed out, and 1 errored (35.5% pass rate). This is a twenty-case gain over the historical 2/62 measurement, not broad parser conformance. Remaining failures include template fixture loading and DOM APIs, task ordering, and adoption-agency serialization. `zero.html` now passes 14/14 after state-specific NUL handling; `ambiguous-ampersand.html` passes all 3/3 after the character-reference, URL serialization, and parser-script write fixes.

**Character-reference and parser-script follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** text/RCDATA references now use the complete `golang.org/x/net/html` entity table, and attribute references are decoded in the upstream tokenizer's attribute-value state so semicolonless names followed by ASCII alphanumerics or `=` remain literal. NUL handling now follows tokenizer context: data text drops NUL and terminates a character-reference attempt, while raw/attribute/comment contexts replace it with U+FFFD. New Go coverage distinguishes those states. Anchor URL query serialization percent-encodes decoded Unicode and spaces. `document.write` arguments now concatenate with Web IDL string conversion, and writes from one classic parser script are combined before HTML fragment parsing, so markup streamed character by character is parsed as one input. The insertion target remains the body append point; exact parser insertion cursor and parser/tree-builder interleaving are still unimplemented. Focused Go tests pass; on the pinned WPT checkout `named-character-references.html` passes 1/1, `ambiguous-ampersand.html` passes 3/3, and `zero.html` passes 14/14. The full refreshed parser-suite result is recorded above.

**Foreign-content/MathML follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** the HTML parser now creates MathML-namespace elements, adjusts MathML/SVG foreign attributes (including `definitionURL`, `xlink:*`, and `xmlns:*`), processes MathML text and `annotation-xml` integration points, and applies foreign-content breakout for `</p>` and `</br>`. Element `nodeName` now preserves foreign-namespace names, and `Element.prefix` returns the nullable prefix. Added Go coverage for MathML names, namespaces, attribute adjustment, integration points, and breakout. Focused WPT `math-parse01.html`, `math-parse03.html`, `html-integration-point.html`, `html_content_in_foreign_context.html`, and `foreign_content_dom_properties.html` pass 5/5; the refreshed 62-case total is recorded above. Foreign-content handling is still partial beyond these cases.

**Document writer and template serialization follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** `document.open/write/writeln/close` now operate on detached iframe documents and the main document, with bounded parser input, visible partial-stream state (a doctype-only stream does not synthesize `<html>` before close), and preservation of manually inserted document children. Document types expose `name`; `document.implementation.createDocumentType` is available. The parser now inserts a doctype before the pre-created root element, and serializing an ancestor's `innerHTML` includes template contents. The focused pinned WPT cases `form-element-pointer-in-template.html`, `insert-into-nonempty-document.html`, and `misnested-form-in-template.html` pass 3/3. This is a targeted slice; the full 62-case parser directory has not been rerun after this increment.

**Parser/script and foster-parenting follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** classic parser-blocking scripts run at their insertion point, and `srcdoc` uses a separate Goja runtime while exposing child globals through `contentWindow`. Parser-created autonomous custom elements now run constructors before insertion and observed-attribute callbacks before insertion; connected callbacks run after accepted insertion. Constructor instances receive their DOM identity during `HTMLElement` base construction so constructor mutations participate in insertion-cycle checks. `Document.body`, `head`, `documentElement`, and `doctype` read the live document tree, and ShadowRoot exposes live `firstChild`/`lastChild`. Template parsing inserts directly into template contents; reading `.content` no longer moves ordinary children appended to the template element. The event loop now flushes iframe load tasks after timer/I/O microtask checkpoints, so asynchronous WPT test callbacks can finish nested-frame tests. The adoption-agency algorithm now completes its outer loop rather than returning after its first restructuring step. Focused regressions cover child realms, custom-element constructors, template insertion, and cycle avoidance. Foster insertion uses the live table parent and reference position; document-target, template-target, custom-element, and adoption-agency reparenting WPT cases pass. Mutations that disabled live-table targeting, restored template-child migration, and restored the premature adoption-agency return were killed by their regressions; source was restored after each mutation. The parser-focused 12-case WPT sample is 11 passed, 1 failed, and 0 timed out; its remaining failure is tentative pagehide behavior. `go vet -all ./...`, `go test ./internal/archtest/`, and `git diff --check` pass. `go test ./...` was blocked by network-dependent Google and WPT-checkout downloads; the repository suite command passed with the pinned local WPT checkout and only the Google-dependent PDF integration test skipped, with the WPT sample result recorded separately. This remains a partial implementation; customized built-ins, full custom-element reaction queues/upgrade semantics, and complete parser lifecycle/order remain open.

**Event-handler IDL/content-attribute follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** `GlobalEventHandlers` properties are installed on the HTML/SVG element, Document, and Window prototypes, and Window handler deactivation/reactivation follows listener registration order. Event-handler content attributes now register through the DOM listener list, compile lazily, track `setAttribute`/`removeAttribute` and parser-created elements, and can cancel cancelable events by returning false. This follows the HTML Standard's [event-handler processing model](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#event-handlers). Before implementation, WPT `inline-event-handler-ordering.html` failed all three subtests because the inline handler was absent from listener order; after implementation, that test and `event-handler-non-content-document-idl-attributes.html` pass 2/2. Added Go regressions cover ordering, return-false cancellation, removal/re-addition, and invalid handler source. A mutation that removed content-attribute listener registration was caught by `TestInlineEventHandlerContentAttribute`; the implementation was restored and the targeted test passes. `go test ./test/dom ./test/js ./test/engine -count=1`, `go test ./internal/archtest/`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. The broader `html/webappapis/scripting/events` directory run produced no report before the 90-second test timeout, so directory-wide coverage remains unmeasured; this does not establish event-handler or HTML conformance generally.

**Event dispatch and legacy body handler follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** `dispatchEvent()` now reflects `defaultPrevented` onto the original JavaScript Event object after listener dispatch; the binding also exposes a MouseEvent constructor backed by the existing mouse-event data path. HTMLBodyElement and HTMLFrameSetElement now reflect the six body/window handlers plus WindowEventHandlers IDL properties to Window. The focused WPT set `event-handler-non-content-document-idl-attributes.html`, `inline-event-handler-ordering.html`, `event-handler-processing-algorithm.html`, and `body-onload.html` passes 4/4. Go regressions cover original-event cancellation state and body-to-Window reflection. Mutations removing the post-dispatch state reflection and the body reflection accessor were each caught by the respective Go regressions, then restored. The earlier directory-wide event-handler run remains timed out; unsupported source-string formatting, body content-attribute reflection, no-browsing-context handler rules, and additional MouseEvent/DOM behavior remain open.

**Inert document and body content-handler follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** body/frameset event-handler content attributes now occupy Window's handler listener slot, compile lazily, and preserve dispatch order through IDL replacement and attribute removal. Template content fragments now belong to their document's shared inert template-contents document; detached HTML documents expose `defaultView === null`, and inline handler attributes do not compile in detached or inert documents. ParentNode's `children`, `firstElementChild`, `lastElementChild`, and `childElementCount` are installed on Element, Document, and DocumentFragment; `children` is live. Pinned WPT `event-handler-no-browsing-context.html` passes all 12 subtests, and combined no-context, cancellation, body-load, and scripting-disabled handler WPTs pass 4/4 files. Removing the browsing-context gate made the no-context WPT fail 6/12 subtests; removing ParentNode accessors made its template-content cases fail 4/12. Both mutations were restored. `go test ./test/dom ./test/js ./test/engine -count=1`, architecture checks, scoped vet, and `git diff --check` pass. The separate WPT body-reflection file currently errors before reporting tests because this runner does not finish its asynchronous WebIDL resource load; direct Go coverage verifies an alternate body's content handler fires from Window. Error-handler special signatures/source strings and the rest of the event-handler directory remain incomplete.

**Event-handler source text and ErrorEvent follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** compiled content handlers now stringify as named functions (`function onclick(event) { ... }`); Window-reflected `onerror` handlers use the five-parameter source signature, while element `onerror` handlers keep the one-event signature. Added the ErrorEvent constructor, ErrorEvent fields/prototype chain, and Window's special ErrorEvent callback arguments `(message, filename, lineno, colno, error)`; generic `error` Events still pass one Event object, and a true Window.onerror result cancels the event. Pinned WPT `event-handler-sourcetext.html` passes 1/1, and the seven-file focused event-handler set passes 7/7. A mutation that removed the event-handler function name made the source-text WPT fail 2/5 subtests; restoring it returns the WPT to pass. Go regressions verify Window argument count/order/identity/`this`, cancellation state, and the one-argument ErrorEvent object for element handlers. At this checkpoint, the upstream `onerroreventhandler.html` still timed out in the iframe-backed cases. Full ErrorEvent interface behavior and the rest of `html/webappapis/scripting/events` remain open.

**Window postMessage and MessageEvent follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** Window now provides asynchronous same-window `postMessage`, target-origin filtering, JSON-compatible structured data cloning, and trusted MessageEvent delivery with `data`, `origin`, `source`, `ports`, `currentTarget`, and `isTrusted`. MessageEvent construction and prototype inheritance are also exposed. Before the change, `event-handler-handleEvent-ignored.html` failed 1/2 subtests because `window.postMessage` was missing; afterward it passes 2/2. The eight-file focused event-handler set now passes 8/8, and Go tests verify asynchronous delivery, payload cloning, event metadata, and origin rejection. Mutating the queued event type away from `message` made the delivery regression fail; source was restored. Full structured clone/transfer support and cross-window/iframe Window messaging remain unimplemented, so this is a same-window increment rather than complete HTML messaging conformance.

**CustomElementRegistry.whenDefined follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** `whenDefined(name)` now returns a `Promise`, reuses one pending promise per valid undefined name, resolves asynchronously with the registered constructor, and returns an asynchronously fulfilled promise for an already-defined name. Invalid names produce an asynchronously rejected `SyntaxError` `DOMException`; a missing argument throws synchronously. The Go regression checks promise identity, `instanceof Promise`, resolution timing/value, and the rejection type/name. A mutation that returned a fresh promise for repeated pending calls was killed by the identity assertion and then restored. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/engine -count=1`, `go test ./internal/archtest/`, `go vet -all ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/engine`, and `git diff --check` pass after the implementation; the focused test passes again after restoring the mutation. The pinned `custom-elements/CustomElementRegistry.html` WPT was not run because the repository curator does not currently expose `custom-elements` (and its support inventory still marks JavaScript DOM coverage false); this Go test is focused evidence for the implemented `whenDefined` behavior, not WPT conformance.

**Custom element registry and upgrade follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** the WPT curator and default WPT config now expose/select `custom-elements` as a testharness directory, so partial behavior is measured instead of omitted. `CustomElementRegistry.prototype` and instance identity are exposed; `define()` now validates the potential custom-element-name code points, returns `SyntaxError`/`NotSupportedError` DOMExceptions for invalid and duplicate definitions, rejects constructor reuse and reentrant definitions in the tested order, validates prototype callbacks, converts iterable `observedAttributes`/`disabledFeatures`, and reads form-association callbacks. Registered autonomous elements work with `new` and `document.createElement`; definition upgrades preserve wrapper identity and traverse shadow-including tree order. Blank iframe `contentWindow` now has its own registry object and frame document creation uses that registry. A Go regression covers uppercase-name rejection, interface shape, direct construction, and createElement upgrade; mutating the name grammar to accept uppercase made the regression fail, then the source was restored. Against pinned WPT `11ae8c1179be2dd38070846b01c2e9338ef81770`, `custom-elements/CustomElementRegistry.html` improved from 17/46 to 45/46 passing subtests. The remaining failure is the WPT's constructor-property access-order assertion: expected `observedAttributes` at counter 6, received counter 5; the exact test source and output are in `/tmp/goosie-wpt-custom-elements-registry/results.json`. The iframe interface in this path still shares constructor functions with the parent Goja realm, so this does not prove distinct-realm conformance. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/engine -count=1`, selected WPT runner tests, `go test ./internal/archtest/`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. This is partial Custom Elements coverage; customized built-ins, full reaction queues, form association behavior, and complete realm semantics remain open.

**Custom element movement follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** `ParentNode.moveBefore()` is available on Element, Document, and DocumentFragment; it preserves tree membership, rejects cross-root moves, and dispatches `connectedMoveCallback` for a moved connected custom-element subtree. The Go test checks the resulting child order and one callback, and suppressing callback dispatch made that assertion fail before the source was restored. The pinned `custom-elements/CustomElementRegistry.html` WPT now passes 46/46 subtests after the registry reads `connectedMoveCallback` when `Element.prototype.moveBefore` is exposed. This verifies that test file only; the broader DOM move algorithm, slotting, live-range updates, and shadow-root receiver support remain incomplete.

**CustomElementRegistry.getName() follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** `getName(constructor)` now validates the argument is a constructor, returns the matching definition name, and returns `null` for an unregistered constructor. It is exposed on the interface prototype and registry instances. A Go regression covers registered and unregistered constructors, invalid arguments, and prototype exposure. Returning an incorrect name made that regression fail; restoring the matching definition name made it pass. The pinned `custom-elements/CustomElementRegistry-getName.html` WPT passes (all four subtests); `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. A fresh full curated `custom-elements` directory run against the immediately preceding binary measured 9/183 passed, 142 failed, and 32 errored; it includes failures in custom-element reactions, customized built-ins, construction and upgrade semantics, and registry support. That directory baseline predates `getName()` and must be rerun before treating its aggregate as current. This API closes one file only; TASK-055 remains far from acceptance.

**Custom-element `createElementNS()` prefix timing checked 2026-10-27:** for a registered autonomous custom element in the HTML namespace, `Document.createElementNS()` now constructs with the unprefixed local name and applies the requested qualified name after the constructor returns. This makes `prefix` null and `tagName` unprefixed during construction, while the returned element exposes its prefix and qualified uppercase `tagName`. Regressions cover those values and reentrant direct construction. Applying the qualified name before construction made the regression fail on both constructor observations; restoring the spec order made it pass. The pinned `custom-elements/Document-createElementNS-prefix-timing.html` WPT passes all three tests. Together with `CustomElementRegistry-getName.html`, the focused custom-elements selection passes 2/2 files. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. This does not fix customized built-in construction, general constructor failure/reaction semantics, or the low full-directory result above.

**CustomElementRegistry.upgrade(root) checked 2026-10-27:** registry instances now expose `upgrade(root)`. For roots owned by that registry's document, it upgrades matching unresolved autonomous custom elements through the shadow-including subtree, including disconnected roots; template contents stay inert and roots from another document are ignored. Definition-time upgrade traversal now also skips template contents. The pinned `custom-elements/registries/upgrade.html` WPT passes all five tests, covering direct, descendant, shadow-including, and template-content cases. A mutation that traversed the template content made the WPT fail because elements upgraded before the template's inert fragment was explicitly targeted. Scoped Go package, architecture, vet, and diff checks pass. `new CustomElementRegistry()` scoped registries, explicit registry association/initialization, and full custom-element reaction timing remain unimplemented; this is partial upgrade support, not overall custom-elements or HTML conformance.

**Custom-element observed-attribute callbacks checked 2026-10-27:** definitions now retain the `attributeChangedCallback` captured at registration and invoke it for observed changes through `setAttribute()`, `removeAttribute()`, namespace variants, `toggleAttribute()`, reflected string/boolean properties, inline style mutation, Attr-node attach/removal, parser-upgrade attributes, and cloned custom elements. Added `getAttributeNS()`, `createAttributeNS()`, and tracked Attr wrappers needed to report callback values and namespace. A Go regression checks old/new values, no callback for a no-op write, style mutation, and callback identity after prototype mutation. Disabling callback notification made that regression fail. The pinned `custom-elements/attribute-changed-callback.html` passes all 13 subtests. The focused selection covering `getName`, prefix timing, registry upgrade, attribute callbacks, createElement, parser fallback, and upgrade reactions now passes 10/10 files. A full curated custom-elements run after these callback changes first measured 15/185 passed; the latest full run is 20/185 passed, 121 failed, and 44 errored (10.8%). Callback reaction-stack ordering, callback exception reporting, full XML name validation, and other custom-element lifecycle semantics remain incomplete.

**Custom-element construction and upgrade checks checked 2026-10-27:** `document.createElement()` and `createElementNS()` report constructor exceptions to `window.onerror` and retain an `HTMLUnknownElement` wrapper when construction fails. Synchronous creation rejects constructors that leave attributes, children, a parent, or a changed owner document, and a returned different Element reports `NotSupportedError`; non-Element returns report `TypeError`. Parser/registry upgrades report `TypeError` for a returned different Element. Failed upgrades retain the custom prototype without being treated as successful instances; failed synchronous/parser creation uses the unknown-element prototype. Constructor state tracking rejects repeated `HTMLElement` construction during upgrades while allowing a nested direct constructor during synchronous creation. Definitions with `disabledFeatures: ['shadow']` reject upgrade of elements that already have a shadow root. Unresolved HTML-namespace elements receive HTML element prototypes even in XML documents. Cloned Documents receive detached document wrappers with independent node IDs and bound document methods. `XMLHttpRequest.responseXML` now returns a cached, bounded HTML Document for `text/html` responses, and connected adoption into the active or iframe document upgrades unresolved elements and runs `connectedCallback`. Go regressions cover thrown/invalid returns, attribute mutations, reentrant construction, XHR response documents, and adoption-triggered upgrade. The pinned `Document-createElement.html`, `parser-fallsback-to-unknown-element.html`, `upgrading-parser-created-element.html`, `upgrading-enqueue-reactions.html`, and `upgrading.html` files pass in the focused run; the combined 10-file selection passes 10/10. The latest full curated custom-elements run is 20/185 passed, 121 failed, and 44 errored. Remaining failures include customized built-ins, form-associated elements, broader reaction ordering, document-global error reporting, and other incomplete dependent APIs. The scoped Go tests, architecture test, vet, and diff checks pass; this is not complete custom-element or HTML conformance.

**Custom-element lifecycle callback retention and mutation reactions checked 2026-10-27:** definitions retain the connected, disconnected, adopted, connected-move, and attribute-changed callbacks captured by `define()`, so deleting prototype properties later does not discard reactions. `Document.adoptNode()` invokes adopted callbacks for custom elements through the shadow-including subtree with the prior and new Document wrappers. `append()`, `prepend()`, `removeChild()`, `.remove()`, and `innerHTML` replacement now dispatch connected/disconnected callbacks for inserted/removed nodes, including descendants. A Go regression deletes callback properties after definition and checks callback receiver and old/new document identity. A mutation that re-read connected/disconnected callbacks from the element prototype made the regression fail; restoring definition-captured callbacks made it pass. The pinned `CustomElementRegistry-constructor-and-callbacks-are-held-strongly.html` passes all five testharness tests; the prior focused 10-file selection plus this file passes 11/11. Scoped `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. The latest full curated custom-elements aggregate remains the earlier 20/185, 121 failed, 44 errored measurement and has not been rerun after this focused increment; lifecycle reaction queue timing and full HTML conformance remain open.

**Custom-element WPT aggregate refreshed 2026-10-27:** the current headless binary and pinned commit `11ae8c1179be2dd38070846b01c2e9338ef81770` measured 21/183 custom-elements files passed, 120 failed, and 42 errored (11.5%). The count differs from the older 185-file baseline because the current curator selects 183. Failures still include customized built-ins, form-associated behavior, reaction coverage, and registry semantics. This is a current broad baseline, not an acceptance-level result.

**Customized-built-in construction and reflected reactions checked 2026-10-27:** HTML element interface constructors now serve as custom-element base constructors; `createElement()` honors object-valued `{is}` options, supports direct and late customized-built-in upgrade, and ignores string options. HTML-namespace `createElementNS()` applies a matching customized built-in without adding an `is` attribute and preserves a qualified prefix. Reflected-property setters now dispatch `attributeChangedCallback` through a common change-sensitive path for string, boolean, enum, URL, signed, and unsigned attributes. HTMLButtonElement reflection is wired for its standard button/form attributes. Added a Go regression for button reaction delivery, native-interface inheritance, direct construction, string-option ignore, late upgrade, and namespaced creation. Removing the common reaction notification made this regression fail; restoring it made it pass. The two `Document-createElement` customized-built-in WPTs and six customized-built-in reaction WPTs (Area, Button, Canvas, FieldSet, Style, TableCell) pass. The current full customized-built-ins reaction subset is 7/28 pass, 7 fail, and 14 errors, up from 2/28 before the reflected-interface additions; remaining failures include other missing reflected element interfaces and unavailable HTML interface constructors. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped vet, and diff checks pass. Customized-built-in coverage remains partial and TASK-055 is not complete.

**ElementInternals and form-associated custom-element follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** `HTMLElement.attachInternals()` is installed after the engine binds the parsed document and creates the HTMLElement prototype chain. Autonomous custom elements may attach once; duplicate calls, customized built-ins, and `disabledFeatures: ['internals']` throw `NotSupportedError`. Form-only ElementInternals members reject non-form-associated definitions. The `form` getter resolves the current ancestor or `form`-attribute owner; upgraded custom elements appear in `form.elements`. `setFormValue()` values and FormData values enter form submission, custom field names/values use CRLF normalization, `setValidity()` tracks validity flags and anchor and normalizes validation-message newlines, and `labels` returns a NodeList-shaped result for associated labels. The Go regression covers attachment, duplicate and disabled cases, form ownership/collection membership, labels, validity, and submitted values. Disabling the duplicate-attachment guard made the regression fail; restoring it made the test pass. On pinned WPT commit `11ae8c1179be2dd38070846b01c2e9338ef81770`, `HTMLElement-attachInternals.html`, `ElementInternals-form.html`, `ElementInternals-NotSupportedError.html`, and `ElementInternals-setValidity-normalize-newlines.html` pass; `ElementInternals-setFormValue.html` still has 27/55 failures, and `ElementInternals-labels.html` still fails its association subtest. The current curated `custom-elements` aggregate is 56/183 passed, 115 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (30.6%); the customized-built-in reaction directory passes 28/28. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. ARIA reflection/roles, custom-state sets, complete label semantics, validation/disabled behavior, reaction timing, registries, and broad HTML conformance remain incomplete.

**Custom validity, ARIA reflection, and custom states checked 2026-10-27:** form-associated custom-element validity is shared through DOM-node state with form validation and CSS `:valid`/`:invalid`; `checkValidity()` and `reportValidity()` dispatch cancelable `invalid` events. `willValidate` accounts for readonly/disabled controls, disabled fieldsets with the first-legend exception, and datalist descendants. `setValidity()` clears messages when valid, validates self/descendant/shadow-tree anchors with the correct TypeError/NotFoundError split, and custom invalid state affects owner-form validation. `insertAdjacentHTML()` now runs connected insertion behavior, including custom-element upgrade. `ElementInternals` exposes the tested ARIA string, element-reference, and frozen element-sequence properties. A native-backed `CustomStateSet` supports set operations and mutation-sensitive `:state(--name)` matching. The regression covers validity events, form validity, pseudo-classes, anchor errors, dynamic insertion upgrade, ARIA identity/frozen lists, and custom-state iteration/order/selectors. Against pinned WPT, `ElementInternals-labels.html`, `ElementInternals-setFormValue.html`, `ElementInternals-validation.html`, all three ElementInternals accessibility property/value files, and `state/ElementInternals-states.html` pass. `ElementInternals-role.html` and two accessible-name tests still fail because the harness lacks testdriver role/name functions. The refreshed curated custom-elements aggregate is 66/183 passed, 105 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (36.1%); custom-elements reactions, registries, constructor semantics, and dependent APIs remain substantially incomplete. `go test ./internal/dom ./internal/css ./internal/js ./test/js ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. TASK-055 remains incomplete.

**Custom-element shadow-including connection reactions checked 2026-10-27:** insertion now traverses light and shadow descendants in tree order, upgrading and notifying each custom element when connected; ShadowRoot exposes `removeChild()` and dispatches disconnected callbacks only for connected subtrees. The connection logic no longer depends on a browsing-context flag for custom-element reactions, so connected template-content owner documents receive the required callback. A Go regression covers light subtree connection, detached shadow behavior, shadow connection, and shadow removal. Removing the descendant traversal made the new regression fail; restoring it made it pass. On pinned WPT, `connected-callbacks.html`, `disconnected-callbacks.html`, and `shadow-descendant-lifecycle-order.html` now pass (the latter was already passing at the prior checkpoint); the refreshed 183-file custom-elements aggregate is 68 passed, 103 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (37.2%). Remaining reaction-queue ordering, adopted callbacks, registry behavior, parser construction, and testdriver-dependent accessibility checks remain open. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. TASK-055 remains incomplete.

**Custom-element adoption and ShadowRoot fragment insertion checked 2026-10-27:** tree insertion paths now snapshot owner documents and connection state, then deliver `disconnectedCallback`, `adoptedCallback`, and `connectedCallback` in order when a connected subtree moves across documents. This covers Node `appendChild`/`insertBefore`/`replaceChild`, Element `append`/`prepend`/`insertAdjacentHTML`, and ShadowRoot insertion. Passing a ShadowRoot wrapper to `appendChild` moves its children as a fragment and empties the root, matching the pinned WPT cases. Go regressions cover adoption order and closed ShadowRoot fragment moves. Disabling adoption notification made the ShadowRoot regression fail on its expected reaction sequence; restoring it made the test pass. The pinned `adopted-callback.html` now passes 71/71 subtests, and connected/disconnected lifecycle and shadow descendant ordering files pass. The current 183-file custom-elements aggregate is 70 passed, 101 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (38.3%). `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. Reaction queue semantics, registry behavior, parser construction, custom-element constructors, and testdriver-dependent accessibility checks remain incomplete; TASK-055 remains open.

**Custom-element connection reaction deduplication checked 2026-10-27:** runtime state now prevents a custom element's connected reaction from being delivered twice during recursive insertion when an ancestor's `connectedCallback` removes and reinserts a descendant. Disconnect delivery clears the state so a later connection still reacts. A regression matching the WPT reparenting shape asserts one descendant callback; removing the connected-state guard made it fail with a count of 2. On the pinned custom-elements WPT run, `connected-callbacks-html-fragment-parsing.html` now passes, `custom-element-reaction-queue.html` improves from 2/6 to 3/6 subtests passing, and connected/disconnected/adopted lifecycle files pass. The refreshed 183-file aggregate is 71 passed, 100 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (38.8%). `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. Remaining queue ordering, registry behavior, constructor semantics, and broad HTML conformance keep TASK-055 open.

**Constructor-time custom-element reaction suppression checked 2026-10-27:** custom-element upgrade snapshots pre-construction attributes, so mutations performed by the constructor do not get replayed as initial `attributeChangedCallback` reactions. `customElements.upgrade()` and definition-time upgrade decide connected delivery from the element's state before construction; this avoids firing connection reactions for disconnected candidates connected by their constructor, and preserves the queued callback for candidates removed during construction. A Go regression covering id/style writes and removals inside the constructor fails when upgrade reads the post-construction attribute list; restoring the snapshot makes it pass. The latest pinned WPT run reports `custom-element-reaction-queue.html` at 5/6 subtests (one per-element adopted-reaction ordering case remains), while `connected-callbacks-html-fragment-parsing.html` and adoption/connection/disconnection lifecycle files pass. The 183-file aggregate is 71 passed, 100 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (38.8%). `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. Complete per-element reaction queues, registry behavior, constructor semantics, and broad HTML conformance remain open.

**Per-element custom-element reaction queues checked 2026-10-27:** cross-document subtree insertion now queues adoption and connection reactions per upgraded element. When an adopted callback mutates another queued custom element, its pending adoption and connection reactions drain before the new attribute reaction, while the outer element queue resumes afterward. Attribute reaction lookup uses the upgraded element's stored definition so iframe-defined custom elements participate even when another document's registry is active. A Go regression asserts the WPT callback order; removing nested queue draining fails with the wrong sequence, and reverting per-element definition lookup makes the focused WPT fail. The pinned `custom-element-reaction-queue.html` now passes 6/6 subtests; `adopted-callback.html` and `connected-callbacks-html-fragment-parsing.html` pass. The refreshed 183-file aggregate is 72 passed, 99 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (39.3%). `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. TASK-055 remains incomplete; registry, parser, accessibility, and broad Living Standard coverage remain open.

**ElementInternals shadow-root behavior checked 2026-10-27:** `ElementInternals.shadowRoot` exposes a root attached during custom-element construction, including a closed root, while `Element.shadowRoot` continues to hide closed roots and pre-attached roots are not revealed through internals. ShadowRoot wrappers now use a global `ShadowRoot` interface prototype, and attempting to attach a second root throws `NotSupportedError`. `attachInternals()` now rejects pre-upgrade calls and allows calls inside or after successful construction. The focused pinned `element-internals-shadowroot.html` passes 7/7; removing the closed-root visibility guard makes it fail. The latest full custom-elements run is 73/183 passed, 98 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (39.9%). `custom-element-reaction-queue.html` and `adopted-callback.html` pass. Scoped Go tests, vet, architecture tests, and `git diff --check` pass. TASK-055 remains open.

**Custom state CSSOM and shadow parts checked 2026-10-27:** `document.styleSheets` is installed after late `SetDOM()` binding, functional selector whitespace is normalized in CSSOM `selectorText`, and document `::part()` rules are made available within shadow-tree style resolution. Part selectors apply conditions before `::part()` to the host and conditions after it to the exposed part element. `getComputedStyle()` now reports the `border-style` shorthand when all four side values agree, covering the host style queried by the conformance test. A direct resolver regression checks part state and host state; a late-DOM JS regression checks indexed stylesheet/rule lists and selector serialization. Pinned `custom-elements/state/state-pseudo-class.html` passes 8/8 subtests, and the complete curated custom-elements aggregate is 75/183 passed, 96 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (41.0%). Removing `::part()` candidates makes the resolver regression fail; removing the computed shorthand makes the focused WPT fail. `go test ./internal/css ./internal/style ./internal/engine ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. Broader custom-elements registries, constructor behavior, parser construction, and full HTML Living Standard conformance remain incomplete.

**Reentrant custom-element reaction queues checked 2026-10-27:** connection reactions for a moved subtree are staged in shadow-including order before the parent callback runs, so nested removals, insertions, and adoption can drain the affected element's queue while the DOM operation is still on the stack. Disconnection and adoption callbacks now enter the same active per-element queue, and pending connection callbacks are deduplicated when the element is reinserted before its queue drains. The pinned `enqueue-custom-element-callback-reactions-inside-another-callback.html` and `custom-element-reaction-queue.html` pass 2/2 files; the former now passes all 8 subtests where its first full-suite measurement had 4 failures. Go regressions cover reparent deduplication, nested removal with and without `disconnectedCallback`, and adoption ordering. Removing pending-connected deduplication makes the reparent regression fail with two callbacks. The refreshed pinned custom-elements aggregate is 76/183 passed, 95 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (41.5%). `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. Constructor/NewTarget behavior, registry semantics, form-associated cases, parser construction, and full HTML Living Standard conformance remain open.

**HTML element constructor NewTarget checks reviewed 2026-10-27:** HTML interface constructors now use `ConstructorCall.NewTarget` to find the registered custom-element definition, reject unregistered constructors, reject using the HTML interface constructor as its own NewTarget, and validate that the definition's `extends` value matches the HTML interface being invoked. Validation uses the shared interface constructor prototype for tags such as `ins`/`del` and `q`/`blockquote`. A Go regression covers invalid and valid autonomous/customized constructors; disabling the interface-mismatch rejection makes it fail. Pinned `HTMLElement-constructor.html` passes 8/12 subtests; its remaining four involve proxy `instanceof` behavior and prototype getter timing. The focused custom-built-in interface files for `HTMLModElement`, `HTMLQuoteElement`, and `HTMLTableCellElement` pass 3/3. `HTMLElement-constructor-customized-builtins.html` still fails both subtests because the JS runtime reads the proxy's `prototype` before the HTML constructor can reject its `extends` mismatch; the pinned NewTarget cross-realm/fallback files also remain failing. The refreshed full custom-elements aggregate remains 76/183 passed, 95 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (41.5%), with its 28/28 customized-built-in reaction files passing. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. Correct proxy prototype timing, cross-realm fallback semantics, registries, parser construction, and broad HTML conformance remain open.

**Customized built-in `extends` validation checked 2026-10-27:** `CustomElementRegistry.define()` now throws `NotSupportedError` when an explicit `extends` value is a valid custom-element name or resolves to `HTMLUnknownElement`, including legacy names still present in the parser's atom table. An omitted/undefined `extends` remains autonomous; an empty string is rejected. The Go regression covers the WPT's valid custom names, unknown and obsolete local names, the empty-string edge, and valid built-ins `address`, `ins`, `del`, `param`, `div`, and `button`; disabling the check makes the regression fail. The pinned `custom-elements/registries/define-customized-builtins.html` passes 1/1. The refreshed full custom-elements aggregate is 80/183 passed, 91 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (43.7%); the 28/28 customized-built-in reaction files pass. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, scoped `go vet -all`, and `git diff --check` pass. Registry semantics and broader custom-elements conformance remain open.

**Custom-element name validity checked 2026-10-27:** `isValidCustomElementName()` now follows the current DOM valid-element-local-name character rule, then applies HTML's lowercase-first-code-point, no-uppercase, required-hyphen, and reserved-name conditions. This admits code points such as U+0001 and U+F0000 while continuing to reject NUL, ASCII whitespace, `/`, `>`, and uppercase ASCII. A Go regression covers both sides of that boundary; rejecting all characters in the local-name tail makes it fail. The pinned `custom-elements/registries/valid-custom-element-names.html` passes all 1,975 subtests (1/1 file), including the 110 failures from the previous run. The normative definition is in the [HTML Standard's valid custom element name rule](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/custom-elements.html#valid-custom-element-name). Broader `CustomElementRegistry.define()` validation remains incomplete; the full custom-elements aggregate at this checkpoint was 80/183.

**Custom-element callback conversion and iframe error constructor checked 2026-10-27:** `CustomElementRegistry.define()` now treats a present `null` lifecycle callback as a failed Function callback conversion and throws `TypeError`; only an absent/undefined callback is skipped. The iframe `defaultView` proxy exposes the parent runtime's `TypeError` constructor so same-origin `about:blank` checks can name and compare the expected exception. Go coverage checks both behaviors; accepting a null callback and removing the iframe constructor alias each make the regression fail. The pinned `custom-elements/registries/define.html` now passes all 70 assertions (1/1 file), up from 22 failed subtests. The refreshed full custom-elements aggregate is 80/183 passed, 91 failed, 12 errored, and 0 skipped (43.7%). The changed-scope Go packages, `go vet -all` over the affected packages, and `git diff --check` pass. This alias does not create a separate ECMAScript realm; iframe realm fidelity remains incomplete.

**Scoped custom-element registry core checked 2026-10-27:** `new CustomElementRegistry()` now creates an isolated scoped registry; registry definitions can be independent, including reusing a constructor across registries. `Document.createElement()` honors its `customElementRegistry` option, elements retain and expose the chosen registry, and an iframe's initial `about:blank` document exposes its per-window registry. During scoped construction, the HTML base constructor resolves the definition from the active registry. Go coverage exercises isolation, scoped construction, document registry exposure, and rejects customized built-ins on scoped registries; changing createElement back to global lookup makes the regression fail. The pinned `CustomElementRegistry-define.html`, `CustomElementRegistry-multi-register.html`, `Construct.html`, `Document-customElementRegistry.html`, and constructor-retention WPTs pass 5/5. The registry directory passes 9/32; `initialize()`, scoped shadow-root/document assignment, declarative shadow registry behavior, `createElementNS` registry options, and `setHTMLUnsafe()` remain gaps. A fresh full custom-elements run reports 83/183 passed, 92 failed, 8 errored, 0 skipped (45.4%).

**Scoped registry initialization and declarative shadow cloning checked 2026-10-27:** `CustomElementRegistry.initialize(root)` now associates scoped registries with documents, fragments, elements, and shadow roots; it assigns only still-null descendants and leaves nested shadow roots intact. Registry lookup follows initialized ancestors and shadow-tree boundaries. `upgrade(root)` and clone-time custom-element upgrades now respect the root's registry, including closed declarative shadow roots and nested roots with null registries. Go regressions cover document/fragment/shadow initialization and declarative nested-root upgrades. A mutation that disabled shadow-root association made both regressions fail. Pinned `CustomElementRegistry-initialize.html` and `CustomElementRegistry-upgrade.html` pass 2/2; the registry directory is now 12/32. The full pinned custom-elements aggregate improved from 83/183 to 87/183 passed, with 88 failed, 8 errored, and 0 skipped (47.5%). `go test ./internal/dom ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./test/engine ./internal/archtest/ -count=1` passes. Scoped-registry gaps remain, including `createElementNS` options, declarative shadow registry parsing/serialization edge cases, and `setHTMLUnsafe()`.

**`Document.createElementNS()` registry options checked 2026-10-27:** the third argument now accepts the global registry, a scoped registry, or `null`; it preserves that association for built-ins and non-HTML namespaces, and selects definitions/candidates from the chosen registry for HTML elements. Go coverage exercises those cases, custom construction, and delayed upgrade on connection. The focused WPT page executes all 10 assertions with zero assertion failures but finishes with a testharness-level `Illegal constructor` status, so it is not counted as passing yet. The WPT runner now includes testharness status messages and page script errors in its failure report to make this class of outcome diagnosable. Focused Go packages, `go vet -all` on affected packages, and `git diff --check` pass. The last full custom-elements aggregate (before this change) remains 87/183; refresh it after resolving the harness status.

**`Document.importNode()` custom-element registries checked 2026-10-27:** the options overload now handles `selfOnly`, custom-registry validation, target/fallback registry selection, and deep import. Non-null source associations are retained; null-registry nodes use the selected fallback, while explicitly null declarative shadow roots stay isolated. Imported elements upgrade using their selected registry without synchronous-constructor restrictions. Go regressions cover scoped fallback, retained scoped association, shallow/deep defaults, and rejection of `customElementRegistry: null`. Pinned `custom-elements/registries/Document-importNode.html` passes 1/1. Cross-window imports in `custom-elements/upgrading/Document-importNode*.html` still fail because iframe realm constructors/registry replacement are incomplete. The registry directory is now 13/32; the refreshed full custom-elements aggregate reports 88/183 passed, 87 failed, 8 errored, 0 skipped (48.1%). The `createElementNS` page still has zero failed assertions but the same testharness-level `Illegal constructor` status. Relevant Go suites, scoped vet, and `git diff --check` pass.

**Initialization-time upgrades and detached selector roots checked 2026-10-27:** `initialize()` now upgrades candidates after associating the root, and scoped registries become associated with a document when used by `createElement`, `createElementNS`, or `attachShadow`; this also lets a later `define()` upgrade elements in an imperative scoped shadow root. `querySelectorAll()` now avoids the live-document index for disconnected element roots. The scoped-registry Go regression verifies pre-defined elements upgrade during document initialization and detached-document queries return parsed descendants. Focused WPTs `CustomElementRegistry-initialize.html` and `CustomElementRegistry-upgrade.html` pass 2/2. `scoped-registry-initialize-upgrades.html` has zero failed assertions, including the previously failing imperative-root case, but still ends in the harness with `Illegal constructor` while constructing another registry, so it is not counted as passing. The refreshed aggregate remains 88/183 passed, 87 failed, 8 errored, 0 skipped; registry coverage remains 13/32. Focused Go suites, `go vet -all` on affected packages, and `git diff --check` pass.

**Scoped registry construction during insertion checked 2026-10-27:** custom-element upgrades triggered while moving a subtree, scanning a newly defined registry, or handling parser-created elements now run with that element's registry as the active construction registry. Before the fix, `Document-createElement`'s delayed scoped upgrade threw `Illegal constructor`, marked the candidate failed, and escaped the harness without counting an assertion failure. The pinned `Document-createElement.html`, `Document-createElementNS.html`, and `scoped-registry-initialize-upgrades.html` WPTs pass 3/3; the focused Go regression covers a disconnected candidate upgraded after definition when appended. The refreshed 183-file aggregate is 95 passed, 77 failed, 11 errored, and 0 skipped (51.9%); registry coverage is 20/32. Remaining failures include custom-element reactions, form-associated behavior, parser construction, cross-realm imports, and testdriver-dependent accessibility checks. `go vet -all ./...` and `git diff --check` pass. `go test ./...` still fails only in `test/qc` because its protected `traceability.golden.json` is stale and requires `go test ./test/qc -update`; that file is protected by this task's §9. TASK-055 remains open.

**`setHTMLUnsafe()` and parser null-registry association checked 2026-10-27:** `Element.setHTMLUnsafe()` and `ShadowRoot.setHTMLUnsafe()` now use bounded fragment parsing and the existing mutation admission path; shadow-root markup inherits its registry. Parser-created elements carrying `customelementregistry` and their descendants receive a null registry, including markup setter content, cloned subtrees, and `srcdoc` documents. Adding the attribute after parsing leaves the existing association unchanged. Focused Go coverage passes, and the pinned `scoped-custom-element-registry-customelementregistry-attribute.html` and `ShadowRoot-init-declarative.html` WPTs pass. The full custom-elements aggregate is now 98/183 passed, 74 failed, 11 errored, and 0 skipped (53.6%); the registry directory is 23/32. The XHTML variant of the attribute test still fails on XML registry association and XML fragment parsing. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./test/engine ./test/wpt ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` pass. TASK-055 remains open.

**Scoped-registry identity across adoption checked 2026-10-27:** createElement-created nodes now retain their default or explicit registry association even while detached; initialization materializes inherited associations, and adoption remaps document-global associations to the destination document while preserving scoped and explicitly null associations. Parser-null locks survive adoption; cloning copies registry identity and null-lock state. The pinned `element-mutation.html`, `registries/element-mutation.html`, `registries/scoped-registry-append.html`, `registries/Document-createElement.html`, and the HTML `customelementregistry` attribute WPT pass. Temporarily removing adoption transfer caused four scoped-registry append subtests to fail; restoring it returned that file to green. Refreshed pinned aggregate remains 98/183 passed, 74 failed, 11 errored, 0 skipped (53.6%); registries are 23/32. The XHTML attribute variant still fails 4/4 because parsed XML nodes do not receive the null association and XML fragment parsing rejects the fixture. Focused Go packages (`internal/js`, `test/js`, `test/dom`, `test/engine`, `test/wpt`, `internal/archtest`) pass; `go vet -all ./...` and `git diff --check` pass. TASK-055 remains open.

**XHTML custom-element registry parsing checked 2026-10-27:** `Runtime.SetDOM()` now captures parser-time `customelementregistry` associations when the runtime is bound after document parsing, with a per-document guard that prevents later binds from treating script-added attributes as parser state. `Element.setHTMLUnsafe()` now uses bounded HTML fragment parsing even in an XML document, while ordinary XML `innerHTML` remains on the strict XML fragment parser. A Go regression creates an XHTML document, binds it to a preexisting runtime, and checks null-registry parsing plus default and explicit-null `setHTMLUnsafe()` behavior. Removing the bind-time registry scan makes that regression fail; restoring the scan makes it pass. The pinned XHTML attribute WPT now passes, and the refreshed Custom Elements run is 98/183 passed, 73 failed, 12 errored, 0 skipped (53.6%); registry coverage is 24/32. Failures in custom-element construction, reactions, cross-realm imports, and other APIs remain; this directory run is not a Task 55 acceptance gate. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./test/engine ./test/wpt ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` pass. TASK-055 remains open.

**Editing command element deletion checked 2026-10-27:** `document.execCommand("delete")` now delegates selected-content removal to the DOM Range deletion algorithm, so fully selected element subtrees are removed as well as partial text. The editing selection collapses at the original start text offset when that text node survives, preserving the caret behavior already covered by cross-node deletion. A Go regression verifies selected subtree removal, the collapsed caret, and one bubbling `input` event. The regression failed before the implementation because only text was cleared; temporarily skipping range deletion made it fail again, and restoring deletion made it pass. Focused editing-command and Range deletion Go tests pass. No upstream execCommand WPT file was run in this checkpoint; collapsed-formatting toggles, nested editing hosts, IME, and caret rendering remain open. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./test/engine ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` pass. TASK-055 remains open.

**Collapsed editing-format commands checked 2026-10-27:** collapsed `bold`, `italic`, and `underline` commands now toggle per-document typing state based on the selection's current formatting context. `insertText` wraps inserted text in enabled formatting, supports simple unformatted insertion inside a direct text child of a matching formatting element, and applies enabled formatting at an element child boundary. The Go regression checks toggling bold on/off, toggling off inside an existing `<b>`, caret placement, and formatted insertion at an element boundary. It failed before the change; forcing the typing-state calculation off made it fail again, and restoring the implementation made it pass. Selected-text formatting and selected-element deletion regressions remain green. Complex nested inline splitting, full editing-host behavior, upstream editing WPT coverage, IME, and caret rendering remain open. `go test ./internal/js ./test/js ./test/dom ./test/engine ./internal/archtest/ -count=1`, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` pass. TASK-055 remains open.

**Nested inline formatting preservation checked 2026-10-27:** when a collapsed bold toggle is turned off inside nested inline markup, `insertText` now splits the matching formatting ancestor and each nested inline wrapper along the caret path. The prefix and suffix retain their nested markup and attributes; cloned right-side wrappers drop duplicate `id` attributes, and inherited italic/underline formatting is cloned around the inserted text. The regression uses `<b id class><i data-kind>abc</i><u>end</u></b>` and checks the exact tree after inserting at the middle caret. The pre-fix result left inserted text bold and placed it at the wrong caret offset; a mutation that rejects nested ancestors makes the regression fail. The focused JS, DOM, engine, and architecture packages pass, along with `go vet -all ./...` and `git diff --check`. Other editing cases (complex mixed selections, block splitting, nested hosts, WPT editing coverage, IME, and caret rendering) remain open; TASK-055 remains open.

**Nested formatting ancestry follow-up checked 2026-10-27:** the split now distinguishes inherited formatting that is inside the element being split from formatting that remains outside it. It clones formatting only when the split would otherwise remove that style, avoiding redundant nested wrappers for the reverse `<i><b>…</b></i>` order. The Go regression covers both nesting orders, cloned attributes except duplicate `id`, preserved trailing children, and caret offset. Focused editing-command and Range tests pass; this remains Go regression evidence only, with no upstream editing WPT run in this checkpoint. Block splitting, mixed selections, nested editing hosts, IME, caret rendering, and broad editing WPT coverage remain open. TASK-055 remains open.

**Repository suite after custom-element changes checked 2026-10-26:** `go test ./... -skip '^TestPrintPDFCommandFlag$'` with the pinned local WPT checkout passed all Go packages through `test/viewsource`, including the updated JS, DOM, engine, gate, and architecture packages. The default `test/wpt` `TestWPTSuite` did not finish: Go's 10-minute package timeout fired while the runner was still executing sequential testharness pages in `runTestHarness` (`test/wpt/testharness.go:254`). The process emitted no aggregate WPT report, so this is an incomplete full-suite measurement, not a WPT failure count. The focused custom-elements WPT above completed separately. Broad WPT runs need per-directory or resumable reporting so one slow corpus run does not erase already collected results.

**Parser iframe lifecycle recheck, 2026-10-26:** with a headless binary rebuilt from the current worktree and the pinned WPT commit above, `html/syntax/parsing/adoption-agency-reparenting.html` and `doctype-system-identifier-distinction.html` now both pass (2/2 files, no failures/errors/skips). This closes the earlier targeted reports that these cases stalled waiting for nested iframe lifecycle events. The full 62-case parser directory has not been rerun after this recheck; retain its last measured 45/62 result until a complete refresh.

**Doctype mode follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** parsed doctypes retain their name/public/system identifiers, and documents expose `compatMode` from parser standards-vs-quirks state. The parser distinguishes missing and empty system identifiers for the common HTML 4.01 Frameset/Transitional cases and records limited-quirks mode for a non-empty identifier; additional legacy doctype public-ID cases still need coverage. The focused `empty-doctype-ids.html` WPT passes. At this measurement, `doctype-system-identifier-distinction.html` was unverified because its assertions waited for nested iframe `load`; Go parser tests covered empty, missing, and non-empty identifiers. `go test ./...`, `go vet -all ./...`, and `git diff --check` passed. Mutating valid public doctypes to quirks mode caused `TestHTMLDoctypeModeAndIdentifiers` to fail as expected; the implementation was restored and the test passes.

**Adoption-agency identity follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** the fast path for an inactive current formatting element now checks that exact node's identity in the active-formatting list. The prior check only tested whether any same-named element was active, which could select an unrelated outer `<code>` and reparent it. The focused Go regression catches that defect, and `adoption_agency_check_the_end_tag_name.html` passes 1/1. Mutating the code back to the tag-wide check made the Go regression fail; restoring the identity check made it pass. At this measurement, `adoption-agency-reparenting.html` was unverified because its assertions waited for nested iframe `load`; the rest of the adoption-agency algorithm still requires upstream coverage. After this increment, `go test ./...`, `go vet -all ./...`, `go test ./internal/archtest/`, and `git diff --check` passed. The 62-case parser suite remained at its last measured 22/62 pending refresh.

**Parser suite and encoding follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** a fresh run of all 62 pinned `html/syntax/parsing` tests completed with 27 passed, 17 failed, and 18 timed out/errored (43.55% pass rate). This is a five-case improvement over the previous 22/62 run; nested rendered-document/iframe tests still account for multiple timeouts and errors. `Document.characterSet` now exposes the document's encoding label; the parser recognizes valid `meta charset` first and a valid `http-equiv=content-type` charset declaration as fallback, using HTML encoding-label lookup. `document.write()` copies the parsed encoding to the live document. Added Go coverage for label precedence and malformed declarations. Against the rebuilt headless binary, `meta-inhead-insertion-mode.html` and `quotes-in-meta.html` pass 2/2. The response path now decodes the byte stream using HTML encoding sniffing, including BOM precedence/removal, transport charset labels, and in-document declarations. Incremental encoding restart and propagation of the selected transport/BOM label to `Document.characterSet` remain incomplete.

**Element-interface follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** unknown, non-custom HTML element names now receive the `HTMLUnknownElement` prototype while hyphenated custom-element names remain on `HTMLElement`; foreign-namespace and XML elements use `Element`. The pinned `Object-prototype-names.html` WPT, which checks both HTML names inherited from `Object.prototype` and XML namespace creation, passes 2/2 assertions. Go prototype-chain tests and repository-wide gates pass after this change.

**Parser suite refreshed after encoding and element-interface changes, 2026-10-26:** all 62 pinned parser cases now report 30 passed, 14 failed, and 18 errored/timeouts (48.4%). The three added passes since the 27/62 measurement are `meta-inhead-insertion-mode.html`, `quotes-in-meta.html`, and `Object-prototype-names.html`. Remaining direct implementation failures include deferred-script/DOMContentLoaded task ordering and SVG script closing/self-closing handling. The template fixture failures report inability to create a rendered document, and 18 cases time out in this runner; therefore 48.4% is a parser-directory measurement, not a full HTML conformance score.

**Deferred-script and event-loop follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** external classic scripts with `defer` now execute after parser-inserted classic scripts. `Runtime.Tick()` executes one ready timer task per event-loop turn, so nested zero-delay timers wait for a later turn. Timers created by a deferred script are tracked; the first ready deferred-script timer task is processed before the document lifecycle transition, preserving DOMContentLoaded ordering for the pinned test. Go timer/engine tests pass, and `DOMContentLoaded-defer.html` passes 1/1. This does not yet implement the full parser script execution algorithm, async/module script scheduling, or queued lifecycle task sources.

**Parser suite refreshed after deferred-script ordering, 2026-10-26:** the full pinned 62-case parser suite reports 31 passed, 13 failed, and 18 timed out/errored (50.0%). The new pass is `DOMContentLoaded-defer.html`; the remaining direct behavior failure in this suite is `unclosed-svg-script.html`. Template cases that load auxiliary documents still fail because this runner cannot create a rendered document, and 18 testharness cases time out; 50.0% is a parser-directory result, not a full HTML conformance score.

**SVG script processing follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** SVG script nodes now record parser eligibility only when a matching `</script>` closes the current SVG script, or when the start tag carries the foreign-content self-closing flag. The engine skips unclosed/breakout-closed SVG scripts and resolves SVG `href`/`xlink:href` as external script sources. `unclosed-svg-script.html` passes all 5/5 assertions; a Go parser regression covers normal end tag, EOF via `</svg>`, self-closing, and HTML breakout. The then-current pinned parser measurement was 32/62.

**HTML byte decoding follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** `Response.Text()` now uses the HTML encoding sniffer and decoder from `golang.org/x/net/html/charset`, replacing the ad hoc `charset=` substring search and Windows-1252-only conversion. UTF-8 and UTF-16 BOM precedence/removal and in-document meta sniffing are covered by Go tests; the existing response charset tests pass. The main network loader, `dump-url`, `goosie-headless`, and the WPT document harness pass the selected encoding label into session construction before scripts execute, so `Document.characterSet` reflects the decoder result and cannot be overwritten by a conflicting parsed meta declaration. The WPT server no longer injects a UTF-8 transport charset into every HTML fixture; explicit `.headers` Content-Type values override its default. The parser API still accepts decoded strings, so it does not model streaming network input.

**Iframe document and template parsing follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** iframe elements now expose stable `contentWindow` and `contentDocument` objects backed by a distinct blank child document; connected-frame loads queue until the current JS script task completes. The engine accepts an embedding-provided, bounded child-document loader; the browser loader restricts this implementation to same-origin child documents and the WPT harness restricts loads to its local test origin. The iframe load lifecycle, a fetched child document, and a parser rule that ignores an `html` start tag while a template is open have Go regression tests. The 12 template parser cases that previously failed on auxiliary-document creation now pass 12/12. The refreshed full pinned parser directory is 45/62 passed, 11 failed, 6 errored (72.6%); remaining failures concentrate on parser reparenting/custom-element interactions and harness/event lifecycle behavior. This does not establish full browsing-context conformance or full HTML conformance.

**`srcdoc` and truncated-doctype follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** connected iframes parse a bounded `srcdoc` into a distinct child document before dispatching `load`, with `srcdoc` reflected on the iframe element and taking precedence over `src`. A truncated `<!DOCTYPE html pu` now emits the doctype token at EOF, and detached documents expose their `doctype` node. The focused engine and DOMParser Go tests pass, and the pinned `truncated-markup-declaration-at-eof.html` passes 1/1. The full parser run immediately before the detached-document doctype binding fix measured 44/61 passed, 11 failed, and 6 errored (72.1%); reparenting/custom-element parser scripts still need parser-interleaved execution, and `no-doctype-name.html` still times out in the harness. Full iframe realms, parser-script interleaving, and HTML conformance remain open.

**Parser script-interleaving follow-up checked 2026-10-26:** the bounded document parser now offers a synchronous parser-script callback, and the engine executes classic inline and blocking external scripts at the parser pause before consuming following markup. Async/defer external scripts remain for the post-parse pass. The runtime binds once to the live document, refreshes structural document references as parsing advances, and retains wrapper/image/iframe caches. A regression proves the first script cannot see a following element while the next script can; image budget, encoding, script order, iframe, engine, DOM, JS, and WPT-runner tests pass. Existing external-script tests now assert parser blocking and document order. The full pinned parser suite remains 44/61 passed, 11 failed, and 6 errored (72.1%): its remaining reparenting cases run scripts inside `srcdoc`, whose child-document parser is not yet wired to an independent scripting realm; template misnested-form behavior and lifecycle errors also remain.

**Repository gates after this increment:** after iframe child-document loading and template parser correction, `go test ./...`, `go vet -all ./...`, `go test ./internal/archtest/`, and `git diff --check` all pass. Earlier mutation checks removed the document.write buffer append, changed MathML element namespace assignment to HTML, and preserved data-state NULs; their targeted tests failed under mutation and passed after restoration. This increment's partial-stream oracle was mutation-checked by forcing EOF completion in `ParseOpenBounded`; `TestDetachedDocumentOpenWriteCloseAndExistingChildren` failed as expected, then passed after restoration.

**WPT gate integrity checked 2026-10-26:** the standalone `wpt-runner` previously returned success after discovering zero tests when its checkout directory was absent or wrong. It now exits nonzero with an actionable message for empty runs. Running it against an empty checkout confirmed exit status 1; the full and focused WPT runs above still discover real cases.

**WPT progress checked 2026-10-26:** `testharness.js` cases now execute in Goosie's JS/DOM engine and the runner collects pass/fail/timeout results; local script fetches remain confined to the WPT server origin. The synthetic pass/fail integration test passes. The runner registers its collector before page tests and preserves WPT's own `done()` behavior. The six upstream `dataset*.html` tests in `html/dom/elements/global-attributes/` pass after fixing live `DOMStringMap` deletion, name conversion, namespace handling, prototype inheritance, setter exceptions, and the `DOMException` surface. This is a narrow real-WPT gate, not evidence of full conformance; expand the run and resolve failures by feature.

**Global-attribute audit checked 2026-10-26:** the upstream dataset tests pass 6/6, the translate IDL/inheritance subset passes 9/9 (including ASCII-only keyword matching, the empty value, DocumentFragment, and shadow-root boundaries), and `document.dir` passes its reflection test. The refreshed `html/dom/elements/global-attributes/` directory reports 139/140 passed, 0 failed, and 1 timed out (99.29%). Namespaced attribute identity and ID selector/localName fixes make `custom-attrs.html`, `id-attribute.html`, and `id-name.html` pass; namespaced `data-*` attributes stay distinct from the null-namespace dataset property. The full `dir-slots-directionality.html` test passes all 17 subtests after adding first-strong directionality, Window ID named properties, ShadowRoot innerHTML/selectors, and basic named/manual slot assignment. `:lang()` now passes HTML, XML, meta, and HTTP language tests after adding CSSOM View's synchronous layout flush and WPT `.headers` support. Template content, document adoption, iframe `contentDocument`, the hidden rendering rule, and the testdriver keyboard input case pass. `cdata-dir_auto.html` still times out while waiting on nested XHTML iframe messaging and CDATA behavior. This directory run is a gap inventory, not a complete conformance gate.

**Directionality and Window audit checked 2026-10-26:** `dir=auto` and default `<bdi>` use first-strong Unicode bidi directionality, while `:dir()` reads HTML directionality. Shadow roots expose innerHTML and scoped selectors; basic named/manual slot assignment feeds directionality and inherited CSS direction. The upstream slot directionality test passes 17/17 subtests. CSSOM View geometry reads flush style/layout synchronously; `.headers` metadata is passed through the WPT server to the document language algorithm. These implementations still need broader WPT coverage outside global attributes.

**Template parsing slice checked 2026-10-26:** Opening a template now enters the in-template insertion mode; its template insertion-mode stack entry is updated as the spec requires. `DocumentFragment` now has the correct prototype and scoped `querySelector`/`querySelectorAll`. Template `innerHTML` parses through a body-owned template context, and in-body `head`, `body`, `frame`, and `frameset` tokens are ignored. Go regressions pass. The six focused parser WPT files have 2/6 file-level passes; the three inline wrapper-token files each pass all non-iframe subtests (5/6, 5/6, and 4/5), while their one failing subtest each requires loading a rendered document in an iframe.
